@@116949611 <p> To enhance its environmental sustainability initiatives in line with global best practices , Dangote Industries Limited ( DIL ) , has embarked on a restoration project of planting 10,000 mangrove trees across coastal states in Nigeria . " We are also committed to creating good climate scenarios where people can live long and their livelihoods can not be taken away from them . " Environmental sustainability is one of the seven Sustainability Pillars of the Dangote Group , and preservation of the environment is one of our core priorities wherever we operate . We are glad to embark on this project " , Adenuga added . @@117110445 <p> SARAWAK holds the record for being the state with the largest land areas planted with oil palm . <p> In 2023 , the size of the planted area for oil palm in Sarawak was approximately 1.62 million hectares , a slight increase compared to the number recorded in 2021 . <p> The acreage accounts for 8.6 per cent of the total Malaysian oil palm-planted areas , followed by Sabah with 1.51 million hectares , or 26.6 per cent . Oil palm-planted area in Peninsular Malaysia amounted to 2.54 million hectares , or 44.8 per cent . <p> The oil palm industry in Sarawak contributes significantly to the state 's gross domestic product ( GDP ) . In 2021 , oil palm contributed 68 per cent of Sarawak 's GDP in the agriculture sector . <p> It is expected to add US$3.6 billion to the state 's GDP by 2030 . It is also expected to create up to 70,000 new jobs in Sarawak . <p> Since 2015 , Malaysia 's oil palm industry 's performance has remained stable and positive . Malaysia exported around 25 million tonnes of palm @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ palm oil industry was valued at around RM137 billion , contributing RM35 billion to the total GDP . <p> Malaysia is currently the second largest producer of palm oil in the world , and it currently accounts for 23 per cent of world palm oil production and 31 per cent of world exports . In all this , Sarawak oil palm industry takes centrestage and is a key driver in strengthening and stabilising the sector 's contribution to the GDP . <p> Oil palm is regarded as the most efficient oil-bearing crop in the world , requiring only 0.28 hectares of land to produce one tonne of oil , compared to 2.24 , 1.49 , and 1.37 hectares for soybean , sunflower , and rapeseed , respectively . This was based on the findings of several studies revealed by the Malaysian Palm Oil Council ( MPOC ) . <p> In addition to its superior economic competitiveness , oil palm 's high yield qualities may be viewed as a guarantee of successful returns . They characterise the economics of the oil palm industry . <p> Rural wealth creation <p> Since wealth @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ 's well-being , increasing wealth is important for increasing well-being . <p> However , wealth creation in aggregate may not improve the well-being of all people in a community . This depends upon the distribution of wealth ownership and benefits among community members and whether the social benefits of the investment exceed the social costs . <p> Although increases in aggregate wealth may not be sufficient to improve the well-being of everyone in a community , increasing wealth is necessary to sustainably increase well-being . The philosophy of ' wealth creation ' is generally central to the plantation development policies and plans that seek to diversify income and business opportunities for the rural community , especially longhouse communities with significant Native Customary Rights ( NCR ) land assets , and contribute to enhancing their living standard and ushering them into the mainstream of modern economy . <p> Yes , Minister ! Increasing wealth is crucial for boosting social and economic well-being since it is derived from locally produced assets like land and agricultural products , which may enhance people 's quality of life . <p> Furthermore , long-term solutions to @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ the efforts to develop and use money efficiently , as people 's income and consumption prospects are dependent on their wealth . <p> of revenue . <p> The independent cultivators in particular have been willing to forgo their traditional way of farming , including dependence on ecosystem services such as river water supply and natural forest products , in return for substantial economic returns from oil palm cultivation . <p> With growth and development of these rural centres , the state benefitted from the ever increasing @@117110446 <h> New and renewed discoveries in our oceans <p> The beauty of a recolonised reef with hard and soft planted corals . -- Photo from Kathy Oakley and TRACC <p> MUCH has been written about the disastrous effects of climate change upon our oceans which we can least afford to ignore . These include rising sea levels , the absorption of carbon dioxide and its acidification effects upon marine wildlife , and the destruction of kelp beds amongst many other concerns . <p> Amongst this ' doom and gloom ' scenario it is refreshing to discover new scientific findings on the significance of Saharan dust on oceanic micro life , coral reef breeding , the rediscovery of a particular Bornean shark and the latest discovery of yet more species of shark , all of which I find of particular interest as I hope do my readers . <p> Saharan dust plumes in the Atlantic Ocean <p> Researchers at Florida State University , USA , have recently revealed their findings from the Atlantic seabed at chosen sites based on the distances from the Sahara-Sahel Dust Corridor running from Chad to Mauritania @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ of iron which have a significant impact on sea life the further it falls away from its source . <p> The iron in the sand particles , blown thousands of kilometres across the Atlantic Ocean , appear to be more bio reactive the further they travel . This was found in their analysis of iron in the seabed cores that they extracted which recorded how much dust had sunk to the seafloor . This airborne fertiliser dissolves quickly in seawater thus stimulating many organic organisms with a supply of nutrients and especially to phytoplankton . The research revealed that a lower amount of this bio reactive iron was found the further from its Saharan source thus suggesting from the rock core samples that marine life had extracted the iron from the ocean in greater quantities . This equated to where , in the Atlantic Ocean , significant blooms of phytoplankton are found through the process of photosynthesis . <p> Renewing life on coral reefs <p> Coral bleaching , through climate change , is a worldwide disaster yet , only a few years ago the late Professor Steve Oakley and his @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ TRACC ) in the Sulu Sea at Pom Pom Island near Semporna in northeast Sabah . There , they bred young corals in empty glass bottles before implanting them in holed concrete crates on the side of a former fishing dynamited , destroyed reef . Today fish and indeed sharks freely swim around the reef thanks to the very hard work of his marine biologist daughter , Hazel , and her colleagues . This innovative way of restoring coral reefs has inspired other marine biologists to try other methods . <p> A very recent study by the Sexual Coral Reproduction Research Centre ( Secore International ) based in Miami , Florida , has responded fast to the maritime heat that struck the Caribbean Sea in 2023 destroying most of the coral reefs there . Conservationists are using a form of in-vitro fertilisation ( IVF ) to breed coral in more than a dozen locations in nine Caribbean centres . By collecting coral spawn during a spawning night , a type of in-vitro fertilisation is used to produce vast numbers of little corals with a wide genetic diversity with the symbiotic @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ are being restored and these have been seen to be more resistant to marine bleaching . <p> Borneo shark ( Carcharhinus borneensis ) <p> Certainly , most people living in the coastal area near Mukah , Sarawak are familiar with this species even if most of us have never heard of it before ! Our Bornean shark was first recorded in 1858 by Dr Pieter Bleeker , a Dutch military surgeon and ichthyologist based in Batavia ( Jakarta ) . He based his observations on a shark caught off Singkawang in western Kalimantan . This rare species of shark is today classified as critically endangered on the IUCN Red List as it is now only found in the waters of the Mukah region . Once it was found as far afield as Zhoushan island , China , Java , and the Philippines . <p> It is a small grey shark reaching between 65cm and 70cm in length and is quite distinctive by its long pointed nose hence ' rhinus ' in its Linnean classification . With a very slender shape , it has two dorsal fins . The front fin @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ and much lower . Feeding mostly on smaller species of fish , it produces five pups per litter , each measuring between 24cm to 28 cm in length . Very little is known about this rare and endangered species and yet more research is needed . <p> New shark species in ocean depths <p> By the mid-1980s , some 360 species of shark had been identified and today there are now 500 identified species with yet more to be classified . This vast increase in the number of species is due to increased human exploration of the deeper ocean chasms in our world combined with newer deep sea fishing methods . Many of these newly-discovered species live at depths of between 300m to 500m and at nighttime they swim upwards towards the surface waters where fish food is more plentiful and thus are more susceptible to commercial fishing . This is seen as ' bycatches ' in fishermen 's nets for they have little commercial value . <p> In 1989 , Australian marine biologists found off the Rowley Shoals ( a group of atolls on the edge of the continental @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ cases which some species of shark lay instead of giving birth like the Borneo shark to live young . These cases looked like the torsos of human skeletons with the central breast bones attached to the rib cages and were dispatched to various natural history museums in Australia and worldwide . It was not until the beginning of 2023 that the home scientists finally put together the pieces of the jigsaw puzzle as to from what sort of catshark these eggs originated . <p> Finally , a new species , the Demon catshark ( Apristurus ovicorrugatus ) was identified . It is thought that this newly-discovered species lives in deep water coral reefs at a depth of 700m , too deep for sunlight to penetrate . <p> The demon catshark was discovered off the coast of Australia . -- CSIRO Australian National Fish Collection <p> Shark researchers share their thoughts and findings , and thus new species are recognised . Two new species of saw shark caught by local fishermen off the coast of southeast Southern Africa , Madagascar , and Zanzibar have now been identified . @@117254486 <p> NOVEMBER 5 -- Polar ice caps are as far away from equatorial Malaysia as they can get . <p> But the interconnectedness of the Earth 's climate system means that changes in one region can have far-reaching consequences in others . <p> As the Earth 's climate warms , sea level rise is projected to increase significantly in coming decades . <p> By the mid-2090s the sea level could be 25.6cm higher than it is today . <p> Countries on the equator are especially vulnerable . The ocean expands due to the increased temperature of the water and this is more pronounced in warmer equatorial waters . <p> Malaysia sits on the equator and has low-lying coastal areas . This makes it and other countries in South-east Asia more susceptible to flooding as sea levels rise . <p> Even small increases in sea level can have significant impacts on coastal communities and infrastructure . <p> Malaysia 's coastal regions are home to diverse and fragile ecosystems , such as mangroves , coral reefs and estuaries . These environments are not only valuable for their biodiversity but also provide coastal @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ . <p> However , the projected rise in sea levels , coupled with the increased frequency and intensity of extreme weather events , poses a significant threat . <p> Malaysian Natural Resources and Environmental Sustainability Minister Nik Nazmi Nik Ahmad said earlier this year : " It is expected that with the rise in sea levels , 111 towns and cities across the peninsula in Malaysia , particularly Penang , Selangor , Johor , Kedah , Melaka and Terengganu , will be affected . " <p> Malaysia 's urban areas -- which are home to around 70 per cent of the population -- such as Malacca , Penang , Johor and the capital Kuala Lumpur , frequently experience extreme storms with heavy rainfall that can cause flash flooding . <p> This study by the Monash Climate Change Communication Research Hub Malaysia node found 70 per cent of Malaysians have experienced floods . <p> In Sabah and Sarawak , sea level rise has a huge impact on local communities . Research suggests that the coastal communities in Sabah lack the know-how to manage the use of their coastal resources sustainably . @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ region . <p> Prolonged flooding and loss of land in coastal areas may force people to relocate . Others might include coastal zone management plans to improve resilience by building communities further inland and on higher ground . <p> Infrastructure development planning might include building and upgrading coastal defences such as seawalls . <p> The Malaysian government is seeking public consultation on a draft new climate change bill , which aims to establish a comprehensive legal framework for addressing the country 's climate challenges . <p> This legislation aims to mitigate the effects of climate change , including the impacts of sea level rise , by promoting sustainable development , enhancing climate change adaptation and fostering collaborative efforts between the government , private sector and civil society . <p> The new bill can play a crucial role in addressing the challenges posed by sea level rise . @@117291898 <h> Church in Spain on the frontline of relief efforts in DANA-hit Valencia <p> As the death toll of the devastating floods that hit the Valencia region last week continues to rise , with 217 dead and many more feared missing , the Catholic Church in Spain intensifies its relief efforts for the thousands of people who have been left without access to basic necessities . <p> By Lisa ZengariniThe Church in Spain is on the frontline of relief efforts in the wake of the unprecedented floods that devastated the southern region of Valencia last week . <p> The flooding began on 29 October , following a flash rainfall sweeping away almost everything on its path . With no time to react , many people died trapped in their cars homes and businesses . A focal point of discussion will be the financing of climate initiatives particularly for developing nations that are often disproportionately affected by climate change , yet lack the resources to combat it effectively . <p> Faith-based organizations attending COP29As in previous COPs , faith-based organizations and religious institutions from various major religions across the world will attend this year 's event to once again advocate with world leaders and governments for climate justice and bold and meaningful action by raising their ambitions in a collective effort to avert catastrophic climate impacts . <p> Among them is the International Cooperation for Development Solidarity ( CIDSE ) an umbrella platform of Catholic social justice organisations working for change to end poverty and inequalities and promoting just and environmentally sustainable alternatives . <p> They will join partners and allies from the Global South and the Global North to urge world leaders to take bold decisions on ambitious , equitable and long-term financing for climate action , including mitigation , adaptation and loss &amp; damage . These will enable all people and communities to respond to the @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ renewable energy systems , particularly in this critical decade . <p> CIDSE ' delegates will take part in a number of official side-events on these crucial issues . <p> The " Faith Pavillion " in BakuFollowing its success of its first edition at COP28 in the United Arab Emirates this year 's COP will also include a " Faith Pavillion " bringing together a diverse coalition of 97 organizations representing 11 different religions worldwide to strengthen the role of religions in addressing climate challenges . <p> The Pavillion features a comprehensive programme , including over 40 discussion sessions exploring the integration of spirituality and ethics into climate action . It will also explore other topics related to sustainable lifestyles , faith-inspired eco-villages , innovative climate financing , and intergenerational dialogue . The Pavillion will highlight the principles outlined in the " Call of Conscience : Abu Dhabi Joint Statement for Climate , " within an interactive framework that will make the Faith Pavilion a hub for transformative ideas and collaborative solutions . <p> Dialogue between science and religions on climate actionIn a significant development , the United Nations Environment Programme @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ present a series of scientific sessions at the Faith Pavilion . Sessions will focus on bridging the gap between science and religion , offering advanced insights into the environmental crisis while grounding these discussions in ethical and spiritual frameworks . The collaboration underscores the importance of integrating scientific knowledge with moral imperatives to address the climate crisis . <p> The four top priorities of COP29The COP29 Presidency 's top negotiating priority will be to agree a fair and ambitious New Collective Quantified Goal on climate finance ( NCQG ) adequate to the climate urgency and scale of the problem , considering the needs and priorities of developing country Parties . However , it remains to be seen whether it will this goal will be achieved , since the threshold of 100 billion per year set in 2009 has never been attained . <p> Another priority theme for COP29 will be the revision of Article 6 of the Paris Agreement , which regulates emissions trading between states . This mechanism continues to be a source of controversy and carbon credits are often seen more as a means of greenwashing than driving @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Loss and Damage Fund formally adopted at last year 's COP28 to help developing countries to recover from the impacts of climate change , as the current funding covers only a fraction of actual losses . <p> Finally , how best to close the adaptation gap , calling attention to the need for innovative funding mechanisms and implementation of adaptation action , including the involvement of the private sector alongside governments and international bodies . <p> Since COP21 summit in Paris in 2015 , whose advances met with ( near- ) unanimous praise , ensuing COP summits have generated a great deal of disappointment and criticism , as States ' commitments have seldom been honoured . <p> Although the stakes are high at COP29 , the challenge of reconciling national interests with global responsibilities is expected to remain a critical hurdle in the new round of discussions in Baku. --Vatican News

@@117393129 <p> CLIMATE change is no longer a distant or abstract phenomenon relegated to the icy extremities of the Arctic , nor a cause celebre championed by a select few . <p> Its impacts are now felt in the streets of Kuala Lumpur and in the homes of everyday Malaysians . With a recent Unicef analysis highlighting a fourfold increase in heatwaves and the devastating floods of 2021 and 2022 still fresh in memory , it is clear that Malaysia is at a critical juncture . <p> The public is eager to confront this existential crisis , but a significant question remains : Can we afford it ? <p> This question goes beyond financial concerns and touches on the structural barriers that hinder our collective action . The obstacles to sustainable living -- whether financial , infrastructural or attitudinal -- risk undermining the will of the rakyat to take meaningful steps forward . <p> However , this willingness often collides with systemic barriers , making it difficult for these efforts to translate into large-scale change . <p> At the heart of the issue is the ongoing tug-of-war between convenience and climate @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ the younger generation ; around 51% of respondents aged 15 to 24 regularly use public transport . This is largely due to its cost-effectiveness and accessibility . <p> However , gaps in coverage and inefficiencies limit its potential as a comprehensive solution . Similarly , many young people are reducing their energy consumption at home -- an encouraging sign of eco-consciousness . However , are these actions driven by genuine environmental concern or are they primarily a response to rising electricity costs ? <p> When it comes to more significant lifestyle changes , such as reducing plastic consumption or choosing eco-friendly products , cost and convenience still play decisive roles . <p> Sustainable options often come with higher price tags or are harder to find , which can discourage even the most committed individuals . This underscores the gap between good intentions and real action . <p> The solution is not simply about individual willpower . It is about creating a system where sustainable living becomes the easier , more affordable choice for all Malaysians . <p> Sustainability , unfortunately , often comes with a price tag that is unaffordable @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ is frequently associated with buying organic , reducing plastic use or adopting renewable energy -- all commendable but often out of reach for the average person . <p> This financial burden extends beyond individual consumption . Many Malaysians are already under pressure to meet basic living standards , making it difficult to prioritise sustainable choices without substantial support . <p> Here , the role of the government becomes crucial . As living costs rise , achieving widespread adoption of green practices requires thoughtful financial support . <p> By introducing subsidies , tax incentives and rebates for eco-friendly products and renewable energy , the government can make sustainability more accessible to a wider population . These policy tools can empower Malaysians to make greener choices without feeling the pinch in their wallets . <p> Malaysia has already demonstrated its commitment to addressing climate change through international agreements and targets . The country has pledged to reduce its greenhouse gas emissions by 45% by 2030 , a significant step towards mitigating its environmental impact . <p> Additionally , as a member of the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change , Malaysia @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ . These commitments lay the foundation for more ambitious domestic policies and actions . <p> The government 's efforts should not stop at encouraging individual responsibility ; they must also create conditions where sustainable living is the default . This requires a multifaceted approach , from stricter regulations on high-polluting industries to investing in green infrastructure . These steps would ensure that sustainable choices are not just available but also convenient and affordable for all . <p> While individual actions are vital in tackling climate change , they must be complemented by broader systemic shifts . The power of individual behaviours , when multiplied , can create a ripple effect , but lasting impact requires governments , industries and communities working in harmony . <p> It is important to recognise that sustainability can not simply be a consumer choice , it must become a societal norm . <p> The current model , which places the burden on individuals while allowing industries to continue with " business as usual " is not just unsustainable , it is profoundly unjust . <p> The time has come for a shift in perspective , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ as a necessity . Malaysia needs bold and decisive action , not just from individuals but also from the government and industries . <p> Policies that make green living the default choice -- such as subsidies for eco-friendly products , stricter regulations on corporate polluters and robust investments in infrastructure -- are key steps towards building a more sustainable future . <p> Moreover , a cultural shift is necessary in how we view sustainability . The responsibility of addressing climate change should not disproportionately fall on individuals , particularly those already struggling financially . Corporations and industries , as the wealthiest and most influential players , have a greater obligation to lead the charge toward sustainability . <p> As Malaysia confronts the realities of climate change , it is imperative that we shift the narrative away from individual action as the sole remedy for environmental degradation . <p> The rakyat are ready to do their part but systemic support is essential for real progress . It is time for the government and industries to take responsibility , ensuring that sustainable living is accessible and affordable . <p> Half-measures are no @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ decisive actions that connect individual behaviours , corporate responsibility and government policies into a cohesive , collective effort . It is time for the system to catch up . <p> The writers are from the Department of Science and Technology Studies , Faculty of Science , Universiti Malaya.Comments : letters@thesundaily.com

@@117485750 <p> RICE as a staple food for half of the world 's population has been a significant icon of Chinese culture for thousands of years . Rice wine residual was also discovered with the relics of the Shangshan Culture . East Malaysia also has a history with rice wine among its agrarian communities . <p> " Long prior to any written records , archaeological evidence indicates the earliest harvesting of wild rice can be traced back to more than 10,000 years ago in China . <p> Advertisement <p> The origins and developments of rice cultivation in China have a very deep root and a long process of evolution , closely linked with technological advances and the unique characters of Chinese life and cultures . <p> " The Shangshan Site Protection Exhibition Hall here features a carbonised rice grain found buried in the soil where several pottery pieces mixed with crushed grains . <p> The earliest rice remains recovered in China have been reported from three archaeological sites : Shangshan in Zhejiang Province , Xianrendong and Diaotonghuan in Jiangxi Province . The cultural remains of these sites are dated to around @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ is set at 1950 , as this was close to when radiometric dating began to be used . The initials ' BP ' stand for ' years before present ' . The use of BP by archaeologists , geologists , and other scientists , refers to radiocarbon ages and results from other radiometric dating techniques . <p> Advertisement <p> " Over 10,000 years ago , the last global and severe cooling event ended , opening a new chapter in human development that archaeologists typically refer to as the ' Neolithic Age . ' <p> " It was an era of transformation , innovation , and awakening . The Shangshan Culture was at the forefront of this era , and East Asian human civilization began its continuous and sustained development for thousands of years . <p> Advertisement <p> " Agriculture is the most important innovation in human history ; it changed the environment , changed specific species , and changed human behaviour . The Jinqiu Basin where the Shangshan Culture was located is an important origin of rice farming , which laid the economic foundation for half of China and has @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ " The discovery of the Shangshan site in 2000 and the dating of the site in 2003 propelled the progress of early Neolithic archaeology in Zhejiang . In 2005 , Shengzhou Xiaohuangshan site was found to have Shangshan Culture remains . <p> " In February to May 2001 , with the approval of the State Administration of Cultural Heritage , the Zhejiang Provincial Institute of Cultural Relics and Archaeology officially began the excavation of the Shangshan site , with an excavation area of approximately 600 square meters , discovering important remains such as house sites . <p> " In order to solve the mystery of Shangshan , the archaeological team sent the excavated charred pottery to the Carbon-14 Laboratory at the School of Archaeology and Museology at Peking University for dating . In January 2003 , dating data was obtained with a measurement of 11,000 years ago . <p> " On November 7 , 2003 , the China Cultural Relics News published an article titled : ' Discovery of Early Neolithic Sites Dating Back Ten Thousand Years in Pujiang County , Zhejiang , ' officially announcing the Shangshan site to @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ the ' Fourth China Environmental Archaeology Conference and the Shangshan Site Symposium ' held in Pujiang , the renowned archaeologists Zhang Zhongpei and Yan Wenming recognised the significance of the discovery at the Shangshan site and officially named it the Shangshan Culture . " <p> This was narrated to the visiting 33 journalists from BRI countries on July 22 , 2024 . <p> " Sedentism is the most important ' revolution ' in human history , which has directly and profoundly influenced population , economy , society , and culture . Compared with seasonal hunting and gathering societies , settlement has rapidly promoted population growth and the expansion of social scale . It also prompted these early settlers to re-adjust the relationships between people and gradually produced complex social organizations and kinship systems . <p> " In China , the Shangshan Culture entered this settled era earlier , so it quickly developed into a cluster of settlements centred on the Jinqiu Basin , with up to 21 sites , which was far ahead of other scattered settled sites discovered at the same time . " <p> Sedentism refers to the @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ to start living in groups for long periods of time . Settling down , picking a place , and living in it permanently for at least part of the year is partially but not entirely related to how a group obtains necessary resources . This includes gathering and growing food , stone for tools , and wood for housing and fires . <p> " Anthropologists defined two different lifeways for people beginning in the Upper Paleolithic period . The earliest lifeway , called hunting and gathering , describes people who were highly mobile , following herds of animals , or moving with normal seasonal climatic changes to collect plant foods as they ripened . <p> By the Neolithic period , so the theory went , people domesticated plants and animals , necessitating permanent settlement to maintain their fields . <p> " Although rice cultivation and consumption had already begun in the early stages of the Shangshan Culture , the traditional hunting and gathering economy likely still played a relatively significant role . Although animal bone remains were extremely rare , tools such as stone balls were associated with hunting activities @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ from the cutting edges of stone tools and grinding surfaces indicated that in addition to rice , barnyard grass plants were also harvested , processed , and consumed . Additionally , nuts , acorns , water chestnuts , and tuberous plants were all wild food resources that the Shangshan people gathered . <p> " In the stone tool assemblage of the Shangshan Culture , the most common and distinctive types are the grinding slabs and grinding stones . These two tools were used in combination for processing food , and were particularly efficient in removing hulls and grinding grains . <p> " The fixed grinding slab was used as a base for placing rice and other foods , while the hand-held grinding stone was moved back and forth to remove the hulls and grind the grains through friction . <p> " Experimental archaeology has shown that the grinding slab and grinding stone were highly effective for hulling and grinding , and may have been used exclusively for this purpose . Trace amounts of rice starch grains have been found on some grinding slabs , providing further evidence to support this @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ genome , but some researchers worry that intensive breeding for high yield and pest resistance has narrowed the genetic diversity of cultivated rice , leaving crops more susceptible to disease and less adaptable to the effects of climate change . <p> Scientists studying rice domestication argue that contemporary rice breeders should take advantage of the handiwork of the humans who transformed wild grasses into the world 's most successful crop . Ancient rice varieties no longer under widespread cultivation could provide attributes to help high-yielding strains to feed a world of nine billion people . There 's great potential in those ancient cultivars . <p> It is hoped that as Sabah revives its Paddy Board to attain food security , there should be a rice museum dedicated to the history of the crop and the people 's culture from whence it emanates in Borneo , Malaysia . Rice has helped China to be a food secure nation and developed to be a civilizational state with its many accompanying cuisines based on accompanying eating rice . <p> The Shangshan Site Protection Exhibition Hall has a construction area of 1,567 square metres @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ It was completed in 2016 and is open to the public free of charge . The exhibition consists of an excavated area of the Shangshan site , cultural relic display , and graphic introduction to provide interactive experience and general education , etc . <p> The exhibition aims to display the discovery , excavation , naming , research and publicity of the Shangshan site to the world . 
@@117485856 <h> Building a Legacy of Conservation and Community <p> Mulu National Park is celebrated worldwide as a premier destination for nature conservation and exploration . <p> While the COVID-19 pandemic slowed tourism across the globe , Mulu has experienced a remarkable renewal in visitor interest , drawing people eager to explore its unique landscapes . <p> Since the pandemic , Mulu 's appeal has noticeably grown , not only due to its UNESCO World Heritage status but also because it serves as a hub for multidisciplinary research , attracting both tourists and researchers . <p> Although the success of tourism recovery has added some pressure on the growing community around the park , Gerstner noted that this will require implementing spatial development guidelines and sustainability measures . <p> Mulu Discovery Centre <p> " This also applies to climate change awareness , along with the need to design adaptive contingencies . Mulu is well-positioned among national parks in Southeast Asia , but there is still much work to be done to manage the site to its full potential , " he said . <p> At the same time , Gerstner said @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ the park 's environmental integrity . <p> He explained that the vision for the national park 's future includes making its trails and activities more accessible to a broader audience , providing softer adventure options that allow people of all abilities to experience its beauty . <p> " We are strategising on making Mulu 's adventures accessible to all while respecting the park 's ecological boundaries , " he said . <p> Gerstner also pointed out that Mulu has the potential to become a training site for other national parks , where local and international conservationists can learn best practices in park management and sustainable tourism . <p> In fact , the national park welcomed over 70 participants from around the world for the 2024 International Showcave Association ( ISCA ) conference . <p> The conference , which took place from Sept 30 to Oct 2 , served as a platform for participants to exchange ideas , explore the latest trends in showcave management , and discuss sustainable tourism practices . <p> Mulu Discovery Centre <p> The event offered a unique opportunity for global networking , knowledge sharing , and @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ note , Mulu 's research initiatives are equally ambitious , as they aim to enhance collaborations with local universities . 
@@117485868 <p> This year 's visitor numbers are closely matching last year 's , and the park management is optimistic that interest will continue to grow . <p> Gerstner said Mulu 's management plan caters to about three times the current number of visitors . <p> " After 22 years of management , it is evident that the national park 's infrastructure can cope with approximately 35,000 to 38,000 visitors maximum before infringing on the remote experience and sense of place . Mulu is somewhat ' artificially ' regulated by the number of available flights and the number of resident guides available to provide the experiences . <p> Interpretive storyboards are scattered throughout the national park . Photo : Sarah Hafizah Chandra <p> " It is important to note that the national park 's accessibility and key products mostly revolve around a guided experience , so the number of guides is crucial for growth , " he said . <p> Noting how guides play an essential role in ensuring that Mulu 's story is told effectively , Gerstner pointed out that UNESCO mandates all World Heritage sites to offer comprehensive interpretation @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ throughout the national park , Mulu National Park strives to provide visitors with an authentic sense of place and a deeper understanding of its ecological and cultural significance . <p> A Nature-Based Approach to Sustainable Tourism <p> Although the term " ecotourism " is widely associated with destinations like Mulu , Gerstner takes a more critical view and favours the term " nature-based tourism " . <p> He described Mulu 's approach as a concerted drive toward nature-based tourism , guided by sustainability guidelines . <p> " There 's nothing truly ' eco ' about humans entering sensitive environments and charging money for it . Our approach is one of measured mitigation and careful management , where we determine acceptable levels of change and know when to adjust our approach in response to natural shifts or degradation , such as those caused by climate change . <p> " We also proactively monitor to detect any human impact before it becomes significant , " he explained . <p> Since 2016 , Gerstner said , Mulu National Park has been programmed with a type of strategic adaptive management . <p> This means @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ be flexible and responsive to changes . <p> This approach allows the park to make informed and proactive decisions in response to challenges like visitor impact , environmental shifts , and climate change , thus ensuring sustainable conservation efforts that adapt over time . <p> " For instance , a rare colony of naked bats near Deer Cave was threatened by visitors using a lookout point , so the pathway was promptly rerouted . We strive to maintain acceptable levels of change . <p> " By monitoring ecological shifts , climate impacts , and visitor effects , we can alter strategies as needed to prevent degradation and protect the national park 's biodiversity , " he said . <p> Gerstner stressed that this adaptable approach goes beyond traditional ecotourism , aiming for a model that genuinely respects and preserves the environment . <p> Monitoring the Environment <p> In an environment as sensitive as Mulu 's , constant monitoring is crucial . <p> Gerstner said a range of technologies is employed to keep track of changes in the park 's ecosystem -- from temperature and humidity sensors in the showcaves to @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Mulu 's weather station , located near Deer Cave , provides critical data for understanding the park 's climate patterns and adapting to shifting conditions . This data-driven approach extends to biodiversity management as well . <p> The national park 's staff carry out maintenance work . Photo : Mulu National Park/Facebook <p> " The Sarawak Forestry Corporation ( SFC ) works closely with our team , using data from camera traps and environmental monitors to identify and protect key species . By keeping a close eye on temperature , humidity , and species presence , we can respond quickly to any ecological disruptions , " he explained . <p> Gerstner said one of the primary goals is to maintain the delicate balance of the national park 's ecosystem . <p> He said this is to ensure that the national park 's ecosystem remains intact for generations to come . <p> " This adaptive approach also serves as a model for other conservation sites , showcasing how technology can support and enhance traditional conservation methods , " he said .

" But not floods . " That , combined with poor coordination among regional and national authorities as well as political decisions taken years ago not to invest in waterways infrastructure , worsened the calamitous loss of life , seven experts consulted by Reuters said . <p> " It was foreseeable that we would have catastrophic flooding here , " said Felix Frances , professor of hydraulic engineering and environment at Valencia Polytechnic University . @@117521824 <p> KOTA KINABALU ( Nov 11 ) : Back in 1987 on a warm night sandy beach in Saudi Arabia , Dr Nicolas J. Pilcher was in charge of a turtle project when a female turtle came to shore to lay her eggs . <p> What 's peculiar about this turtle is that after digging the hole for about an hour , she would leave it behind , without laying any eggs , to look for another spot on the beach to dig another hole . This female turtle would dig two more holes before moving again to another fresh spot . <p> As the clock ticked into the wee hours , the irritated but curious Pilcher , who was in his teens at that time , decided to crawl up behind the turtle to see what the fuss was all about when something caught him by surprise which changed the course of his life . <p> " This turtle was having difficulty laying eggs as it continued to move from one hole to another hole , so I decided to crawl up behind her and was shocked to see @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ dig a nest chamber . <p> " So I lay on the sand with her and helped her to dig a hole . Each time when she used her flipper stump to dig a hole , I would dig down with my hand to help out . Her flipper , my hand . Her flipper , my hand . Slowly but surely we dug the nest and she finally laid her eggs . <p> " Two weeks later , she came back to the beach and I was there to greet her and we dug a nest together and I helped her three more times that year . <p> " Two years later , she came back to the same beach to lay her eggs and I was there on that same beach to give her ' my flipper ' ( my hand ) . <p> " Up to the point that it happened , to me it was just another job that I did . I have been helping turtles ever since and now I 'm known as the turtle guy , " said Pilcher in celebrating Marine Research @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ November 9 . <p> Established in 2004 , the Marine Research Foundation ( MRF ) is the first marine-oriented NGO in Malaysia with its main aim to promote the sustainable use of natural resources with a practical approach to conservation , providing efficient solutions to concerned stakeholders . <p> As Malaysia 's pioneer marine-based NGO , MRF has invested the last 20 years in the conservation of marine wildlife and their habitats , running community-based turtle conservation projects , in-water research and tracking studies and aerial survey projects to support conservation efforts . <p> " It 's been an incredible journey . From the early days as a one man show to today 's team of dedicated Malaysian conservationists , it is amazing to see how much things have changed , " said Pilcher , who is MRF Executive Director . <p> " We have raised over RM30 million over the years to invest in marine conservation and we have delivered on our goals . Turtles are being saved . Sharks and rays are being saved . It just goes to show that with dedication and passion , there is @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ said . <p> MRF runs a number of projects related to biodiversity assessment and conservation , providing management-oriented solutions to government administrations and conservationists . Some examples of MRF 's project include working in partnership with the Department of Fisheries Sabah to develop sustainable fishery practices and reduce bycatch in shrimp fisheries via Turtle Excluder Devices , and deploying time-lapse GPS-stamped cameras to assess the magnitude and distribution of shark and ray bycatch . <p> MRF 's flagship project is saving sea turtles by introducing fishers to Turtle Excluder Devices , or TEDs . For now , Malaysia is the only country in Southeast Asia to use TEDs to save sea turtles . <p> MRF 's senior conservation officer Liyana Izwin bt Khalid said TEDs are saving over 1,000 turtles each year in Malaysia . <p> " Imagine if all trawl vessels used them , we could save over 5,000 turtles each year . This is why we work so hard to get them accepted . <p> " I have to say that our collaboration with the Department of Fisheries Sabah and Sabah Parks are the key to our success @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ makes all our work worthwhile . When a small six-person NGO can alter policy in a country , you know you are on the right track , " she said . <p> Another of MRF 's key projects is investigating impacts of climate change on sea turtles , using laparoscopy to determine gender ratios of turtles on their feeding grounds and linking these to management practices at hatcheries and with climate change . <p> MRF also works on the conservation of sharks and rays in partnership with WWF Malaysia and Green Semporna , and together they are raising the bar on conservation at a national and regional level . <p> MRF 's work with time-lapse cameras is helping design marine protected areas , which have the potential to save over 90 percent of Sabah 's breeding hammerhead sharks .

" We need healthy ecosystems to maintain this functionality so that they can act as a sink of carbon and not become a source for carbon release , " she said. --Vatican News

@@117727783 <p> AS leaders gather for the 29th Climate Conference ( COP29 ) in Azerbaijan , they must face the reality that the climate crisis is also a health crisis for millions of the most vulnerable people on our increasingly warmer planet . <p> To respond effectively , it is crucial to place health at the centre of discussions , policies and funding decisions while also drawing lessons from insights in Southeast Asia . <p> My colleagues at Medecins Sans Frontieres/Doctors Without Borders ( MSF ) work in some of the most climate-vulnerable regions , serving populations who already lack access to basic healthcare or are deliberately excluded from it . <p> The climate crisis is hitting them the hardest . We see this firsthand , with more and more people seeking care in our clinics . We can see how failures in climate action are creating ripple effects that are worsening healthcare outcomes in humanitarian settings . <p> Adamo Armando Palame , an MSF health promotion supervisor in Mozambique explains it this way : " Those who wonder what climate change looks like should come to Mozambique . We are @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ polluting countries . We now have malaria all year round and we are struck by cyclone after cyclone . " <p> Climate change increases the risk of illnesses for vulnerable populations , both directly -- through harm from extreme weather events or the spread of vector-borne , waterborne and human-to-human communicable diseases -- and indirectly , by eroding social and economic coping mechanisms such as livelihoods , healthcare systems and access to water and sanitation . <p> Communities most at risk often have the least capacity to adapt to the complex and compounding impacts of climate change while humanitarian responses to their growing needs become increasingly challenging . <p> In South Sudan , an MSF team observed how unpredictable seasonal patterns and rains forced a usually sedentary community into semi-nomadism for survival , complicating the roll-out of a three round multiantigen vaccination campaign . <p> Most of the 25 countries most vulnerable to climate change and least equipped to adapt are also affected by armed conflict . <p> Recent analysis by the Peace Research Institute Oslo shows that state-based armed conflict is at its highest level in 30 years . @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ , Myanmar , Nigeria , Russia and Ukraine , Somalia , Sudan and Syria , this portends more suffering . <p> Conflict instability and the politicisation of healthcare are compounding factors for communities already at risk from climate-driven disasters , as they struggle with inadequate infrastructure and limited preparedness and response capacity . <p> In 2023 , Cyclone Mocha -- the largest to hit Myanmar in a decade -- made landfall in Rakhine state , where many people have been internally displaced by ongoing conflict and are living in temporary shelters . <p> Before the cyclone hit , thousands of people were already grappling with severe seasonal water shortages . Skin diseases , caused by lack of clean water for washing , accounted for 20% of our patient consultations during the dry season . <p> The cyclone damaged essential infrastructure , and the contamination of the already limited water supply quickly followed . <p> My MSF colleagues prioritised preventing waterborne diseases , distributing drinking water to 9,000 people per week and repairing damaged latrines and water systems . <p> We also resumed our pre-disaster activities , including mobile primary health @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ only a fraction of what was needed and what could have been done . Why ? <p> Weeks after the cyclone , travel authorisations were still blocked , and when they were eventually granted , they were limited to activities pre-approved by military authorities . <p> The military then demanded that aid groups hand over relief supplies for their own distribution , violating the principles of impartiality and neutrality in humanitarian assistance . <p> In the past two years alone , my colleagues have responded to widespread flooding in Africa 's Sahel region and parts of Asia , severe tropical cyclones in Madagascar and Mozambique , and relentless heat and drought that have pushed millions to the brink of starvation in Somalia and Ethiopia . <p> During the same period , we also responded to concurrent cholera outbreaks in more than 10 countries where poor access to clean water , inadequate sanitation and weak healthcare infrastructure were exacerbated by rising temperatures and intensifying cycles of floods and drought . <p> According to the World Health Organisation ( WHO ) , 35 countries experienced cholera outbreaks in 2023 -- 13% more @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ and malnutrition has kept our pediatric wards full across the Sahel region . In Chad , our teams now provide year-round prevention and treatment for malnutrition , instead of only during the seasonal peaks as we did before . <p> From Niger to Mozambique , and from Honduras to Bangladesh , we are treating more patients for malaria and dengue as the vectors of these diseases -- mosquitoes -- adapt to new environmental conditions and migrate to previously unaffected areas due to climate change . This exposes more people to infection for longer periods . <p> Dengue is the most common and particularly concerning . Today , over 3.9 billion people are estimated to be at risk , with that number likely to increase by another billion by 2050 , according to the WHO . <p> Dengue is already endemic in Southeast Asia , and MSF has successfully replicated some of the innovative vector-control methods trialled in Indonesia , Malaysia , Thailand and Vietnam in other regions . <p> In Honduras , where more than 10,000 cases of dengue are reported annually , the transmission potential has increased significantly , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ past 14 years . <p> Traditional chemical vector-control methods are less effective now ; mosquitoes have become resistant to them . <p> Last year , MSF and the Honduran Health Ministry launched new vector-control studies , including a pilot of the Wolbachia method , similar to those conducted in Singapore and Southeast Asia . The mosquito population in a district of Tegucigalpa is being replaced with mosquitoes inoculated with the Wolbachia bacteria that blocks dengue viruses from replicating and helps reduce transmission . <p> Previous studies in other endemic areas have shown that Wolbachia can reduce dengue transmission by up to 95% . The method appears to be environmentally safe , self-sustaining and cost-effective . <p> I hope that trialling practical solutions in Southeast Asia , in ways that humanitarian groups like MSF can replicate elsewhere , becomes a hallmark of the future humanitarian landscape . This region has the potential to offer solutions for areas where institutions are often weaker or less reliable . Such efforts would be a powerful expression of solidarity through concrete action . <p> Other steps should include producing and sharing knowledge to fill gaps @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ the authoritative Intergovernmental Panel on Climate Change highlighted that meningitis , snakebite , leishmaniasis , measles , Ebola and human African trypanosomiasis -- all climate-sensitive diseases and humanitarian health problems -- were under-reported or absent in the latest edition . <p> While health does not feature prominently in international decision-making processes , it urgently needs to be at the centre of all multilateral climate negotiations , policies , commitments and action . This means bringing health organisations and stakeholders to the negotiating table . Southeast Asia can also play a significant role . <p> Finally , coherence on planning for and responding to climate health emergencies is essential since efforts still often remain siloed , with little coordination and coherence across sectors -- perhaps most of all , in terms of funding . <p> A key focus of COP29 will fall on finance , and we already see some countries choosing to cut humanitarian funding and divert that money to climate programming . This should not be . <p> Coherent climate action must fill gaps and enhance humanitarian and development assistance , not be set up to compete with it . 
@@117745397 <h> Supercell tropical cyclones -- hurricanes and typhoons <p> Photo shows Hurricane Milton as it churned over the Gulf of Mexico as a Category 4 storm . -- Nasa photo <p> SUPERCELL tropical storms are initiated by the heat held in the top 30 metres of the oceans , usually above 26 degrees Celsius , and the resultant increase in the humidity of the air . Climate change , it seems , has promoted more frequent and more violent hurricanes than those of bygone years . The Northwestern Pacific Basin spawns nearly a third of the world 's tropical cyclones with wind speeds of at least 119km per hour ( kph ) . <p> Covering this region a specialised meteorological centre has been established in Japan with others in Hawaii , the Philippines , and in Hong Kong . The typhoon season runs from May to November but beware for climate change could alter these traditional timings . Likewise , hurricanes experience winds of at least 119 kph and are generally confined to the Central North Pacific , Eastern North Pacific , and the North Atlantic . <p> The origin @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ first recorded in 1124 , although there is great debate about whether it is derived from the Ancient Greek ' typhon ' or from the word ' tufau ' in Hindi and Persian . The word hurricane comes from the Mexican Mayan God of fire and storms , ' Huracan ' before it was finalised in English in 1688 . <p> What are tropical cyclones and their impacts on humanity ? <p> All tropical cyclones are large circulating storm systems with extraordinary high wind speeds . Consisting of a very low-pressure core , they are surrounded by spiral bands of extremely vigorous convection with cloud systems , as observed by meteorological satellites , thousands of kilometres in diameter . <p> When these storms hit land , several factors contribute to their devastating effects . With their constantly high wind speeds , they can spawn even stronger small tornadoes . The very low pressure in their centres together with the circulating winds create a dome of water , often several metres higher than normal sea levels . Such storm surges normally cause most of the devastation when either hurricanes or typhoons @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ than 20km inland . With torrential downpours of rain , which often causes flooding and triggers landslides and mudflows , they can sweep away vehicles , bridges , railways , and even whole settlements which are often built in coastal locations and thus are most vulnerable to the sheer force of these storms . <p> The Caribbean countries , Mexico , and the USA states of Florida and Texas are most prone to hurricanes and Japan , Korea , China , and the Philippines are most affected by typhoons but fortunately these super tropical cyclones die away when they pass over land or when they reach colder waters . However , sometimes they reach middle latitudes and merge with pre-existing depressions , which cause them to further deepen and become extremely vigorous storms reaching Western Europe with strong winds and torrential rain as experienced in late September and late October this year . These areas suffered severe flooding and in some countries the loss of lives . <p> El Nino and La Nina years dictate the paths that typhoons follow . Owing to modifications in the Pacific 's atmospheric belts @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Korea experience fewer September to November tropical cyclones . During La Nina years , with a shift in a high-pressure ridge across the western Pacific Ocean , typhoons often hit China and the Philippines . <p> Only once in the 30 years I have been visiting Borneo did I experience the tail end of a typhoon which had previously caused extensive damage in the Philippines . Whilst eating an evening meal in a coastal hotel on the outskirts of Kota Kinabalu , in Sabah -- the so-called ' Land below the Wind ' -- a violent storm erupted with extremely strong winds which penetrated the shutters throwing the windows wide open . These winds lasted for about five to 10 minutes , whipping away tablecloths , and throwing plates and table decorations onto the floor . Outside rain pounded down and the nearby seas raged . My heart went out to the hotel staff in cleaning up the chaos and especially to the inhabitants in the nearby waterside villages built on stilts over the sea . Their homes were totally wrecked . <p> The strength of a typhoon can be @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ intensity in the table . <p> Recent devastating hurricanes in the USA <p> In September and October this year , two hurricanes named Helene and Milton devastated Florida , ' The Sunshine State ' of the USA . Milton was the fourth major hurricane to hit this state in the last 14 months and originated from the Gulf of Mexico and then drifted over the state to the Atlantic Ocean . It reached Category 5 on the Saffir-Simpson Hurricane wind scale , with wind strengths of greater than 252kph and releasing 126 tornadoes in the Caribbean before moving north to Florida . There , buildings were demolished , record rainfalls were recorded , and nearly four-metre-high sea waves were observed . High rise cranes crashed down on buildings , roofs were torn away , power lines were brought down , with electricity flashes lighting up the night skies as transformers exploded , and thousands of homes flooded . <p> The Tropicana Field baseball stadium in St Petersburg , Florida was being used as a shelter for first responders until its roof was ripped off . -- AFP photo <p> Fortunately @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ had evacuated their boarded up homes and properties and moved further inland away from the forecasted track of the hurricane . Hurricane Helene passed over Florida only two weeks previously causing tens of million dollars damage . It has been stated that such a forceful hurricane releases heat energy equivalent to a 10 megaton nuclear bomb every 20 minutes , which is more than all the energy used worldwide at a given time . <p> Effects of hurricanes and typhoons upon bird migrations <p> The eyes of typhoons or hurricanes usually experience gentle winds and clear skies but are surrounded by a ring of the most vicious of winds and torrential rainfall . As a severe tropical cyclone moves , thousands of birds travel in the eye of the storm until the storm weakens over land . Such birds are large seabirds such as the Frigate bird , which can travel long distances before the storm subsides . However smaller birds caught up in tropical storms perish through absolute tiredness and lack of food . Other seabirds such as Petrels actually track such tropical cyclones for these storms churn up @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ . <p> As Caribbean hurricanes subside ; after drifting across the Atlantic Ocean , several American species of small warbler birds have landed in the Orkney Islands in Scotland and even in the far southwest of the UK on the Isles of Scilly attracting dozens of fervent ornithologists . <p> Such violent atmospheric events are beyond our control , but we can try to reduce their frequency and strength by our nations ' pledges to reduce carbon emissions thereby lessening the effects of climate change in terms of global temperature increases . Unless we as human beings make a sustained effort , we can expect an increase in such tropical cyclones in future years with increasing damage to our properties and sadly even greater loss of lives .

@@117745455 <p> Its flagship project is saving sea turtles by being the first to introduce the Turtle Excluder Devices , or TEDs , to fishermen to reduce bycatch , making Malaysia the only country in Southeast Asia to use TEDs to save sea turtles . <p> Advertisement <p> " We have delivered our goals . Why did you pick Sabah as your base ? <p> Dr Nick : @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ The political landscape in terms of NGOs operating in Malaysia was such that Sabah was the most welcoming State in the country -- and continues to be to this day . <p> Sabah government agencies are happy to work with NGOs and they appreciate that NGOs can achieve some things that they do not have the resources for . <p> MRF works closely with Sabah Fisheries , Sabah Parks and Sabah Wildlife , and all of our relationships are positive and based on trust and friendships . <p> It is a wonderful environment to work in . <p> My wife is also Sabahan , and that played quite a key role also . Her family have adopted me as one of the family , and welcomed me in the most amazing way . <p> Our " balik kampong " days to Papar are always epic and memorable , and I am honoured to have been lovingly welcomed . <p> Lastly , the turtles . <p> The turtles in Sabah are what drew me here in the first place , and still are the largest part of my professional life . @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ turtles in the sea , where they spend the largest part of their lives , and this has been a key MRF mission since day one . <p> MRF is the first ever marine-oriented NGO to be established in Sabah -- indeed in all of Malaysia -- and this is a legacy for us . We were the first . <p> We will always be the first , and we are proud that Sabah was the first to recognise the need for an MRF . <p> Daily Express : So what do you now understand about the marine diversity of the region ? <p> Dr Nick : Mostly that it still lags far behind terrestrial species and habitats in terms of recognition and need for preservation . <p> Only in recent years has the public really started to pay attention , while our attention to tigers and rainforests and orangutan date back more than thirty years . <p> It is very much an " out of sight , out of mind " situation , with people not realising just how much land-based activities can impact the ocean . But this @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ years ago , the ocean was a place where we just went fishing . Today , many more people understand its importance . <p> Also , things like overfishing threaten the very resource we all depend on , and it goes unmanaged in many ways . <p> Dr Nick speaking at MRF 's 20th anniversary event at Spice Island . <p> We do n't have enough direct management action to prevent overfishing , or catching unwanted species , known as bycatch . Sea turtles are a species that gets caught by the thousands in fisheries every year in Sabah -- yes , thousands , and each year ! And we are not yet implementing measures that would reduce this . <p> Controlling fishing is not about telling someone they ca n't earn a living . It 's about telling someone that that action needs to be managed so we can all fish tomorrow , and it is time for all to come on board with this mindset . <p> Fishers know the sea is their rice bowl -- we just need it to be better regulated . <p> Bottom line @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ our marine environments because we depend on them . <p> Tourism depends on healthy coral reefs full of turtles and sharks and beautiful fish . <p> Our economy depends on sustainable fisheries . There are so many ways in which the oceans support humankind that it is amazing to me that there is not more effort is invested in preserving these environments . <p> Daily Express : You 're reportedly goal-and project-oriented with a reputation that has not failed to deliver a single of many projects over 20 years , how did you do that ? <p> Dr Nick : This is true . <p> We do n't fail . Our motto is " excuses are not part of the solution " and every project we undertake on behalf of a donor or a client has been successful . <p> I think a key reason we did this is we are a team of very passionate and dedicated conservation practitioners , but we are not rabid " chain yourself to a turtle " type of people . <p> We are pragmatic , and we are results driven , and we @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ us too persistent , but we do n't want to waste funds doing things that are never going to go anywhere . <p> And while we are worried about turtles , and sharks and rays , and dugongs , we are also very cognisant of the fishing communities we work with . <p> We have some amazing trust among fishing communities who invite us into their homes and work with us as if we were family . My wife has been my support when I have had doubts , and kept out home growing while I was busy saving the planet . 
@@117745462 <p> INCREASING pollution and unsustainable practices are threatening Malaysia 's marine wildlife , placing the future of our blue economy -- an economy built on the sustainable use of ocean resources -- in jeopardy . <p> In recent years the signs of distress have become impossible to ignore . Similarly , along the beaches of Langkawi , dolphins and dugongs have washed ashore , either entangled in fishing nets or suffering from the toxic effects of oil spills and water contamination . <p> Marine pollution , particularly from plastics , has reached critical levels . " For communities like his , the loss of marine wildlife is not just an environmental issue ; it 's an economic one . <p> Despite these challenges , there is hope . This is not just a job for the government -- it requires a nationwide effort . Each time we choose to recycle , refuse a plastic bag , or participate in a beach cleanup , we are contributing to the solution . <p> The health of Malaysia 's marine ecosystems is not just an environmental issue , it 's also an economic one . If we want to ensure a prosperous future for both our marine wildlife and the millions of Malaysians who depend on the ocean , we must act now . Rising temperatures are like hitting the accelerator on that process , speeding up the biological interactions that produce methane . Heavy rains , meanwhile , trigger flooding that causes wetlands to expand . Scientists had long projected wetland methane emissions would rise as the climate warmed , but from 2020 to 2022 , air samples showed the highest methane concentrations in the atmosphere since reliable measurements began in the 1980s . <p> Four studies published in recent months say that tropical wetlands are the likeliest culprit for the spike , with tropical regions contributing more than 7 million tonnes to the methane surge over the last few years . " Methane concentrations are not just rising , but rising faster in the last five years than any time in the instrument record , " said Stanford University environmental scientist Rob Jackson , who chairs the group that publishes the five-year Global Methane Budget , last @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ as the source of a large increase . Scientists further analysed distinct chemical signatures in the methane to determine whether it came from fossil fuels or a natural source -- in this case , wetlands . <p> The Congo , Southeast Asia and the Amazon and southern Brazil contributed the most to the spike in the tropics , researchers found . Data published in March 2023 in Nature Climate Change shows that annual wetland emissions over the past two decades were about 500,000 tonnes per year higher than what scientists had projected under worst-case climate scenarios . <p> Capturing emissions from wetlands is challenging with current technologies . " We should probably be a bit more worried than we are , " said climate scientist Drew Shindell at Duke University , The La Nina climate pattern that delivers heavier rains to parts of the tropics appeared somewhat to blame for the surge , according to one study published in September in the journal Proceedings of the National Academy of Sciences . <p> For countries trying to tackle climate change , " this has major implications when planning for methane and @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ an atmospheric chemist at North Carolina State University who led the study on La Nina impacts . <p> If wetland methane emissions continue to rise , scientists say governments will need to take stronger action to hold warming at 1.5 C ( 2.7 F ) , as agreed in the United Nations Paris climate accord . <p> Water world <p> Methane is 80 times more powerful than carbon dioxide ( CO2 ) at trapping heat over a timespan of 20 years , and accounts for about one-third of the 1.3 degrees Celsius ( 2.3 F ) in warming that the world has registered since 1850 . Unlike CO2 , however , methane washes out of the atmosphere after about a decade , so it has less of a long-term impact . More than 150 countries have pledged to deliver 30 per cent cuts from 2020 levels by 2030 , tackling leaky oil and gas infrastructure . <p> But scientists have not yet observed a slowdown , even as technologies to detect methane leaks have improved . Methane emissions from fossil fuels have remained around a record high of 120 million @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ 's 2024 Global Methane Tracker report . Satellites have also picked up more than 1,000 large methane plumes from oil and gas operations over the past two years , according to a UN Environment Programme report published on Friday , but the countries notified responded to just 12 leaks . <p> Some countries have announced ambitious plans for cutting methane . <p> China last year said it would strive to curb flaring , or burning off emissions at oil and gas wells . President Joe Biden 's administration finalised a methane fee for big oil and gas producers last week , but it is likely to be scrapped by the incoming presidency of Donald Trump . <p> The Democratic Republic of Congo 's environment minister Eve Bazaiba told Reuters on the sidelines of the UN climate summit COP29 that the country was working to assess the methane surging from the Congo Basin 's swampy forests and wetlands . Congo was the largest hotspot of methane emissions in the tropics in the 2024 methane budget report . <p> " We do n't know how much methane is coming off our wetlands @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ bring in those who can invest in this way , also to do the monitoring to do the inventory , how much we have , how we can also exploit them . " -- Reuters

So many people in many countries on our Flyway celebrate with you . " <p> " China has contributed globally and nationally to the protection of Nature . " <p> Addressing the President of the Summit on Monday , he underscored the power shift from nation-states to international multilateral organizations that has taken place in the last century . <p> A 21st-century shiftFollowing the Second World War , Cardinal Parolin pointed out the increase in the number of countries which gained independence and changed the makeup of the political world scene . <p> As a result , international multilateral organizations " appear to be encountering challenges in responding to the demands of the 21st century . " <p> As a solution , the Secretary of State called for a change , a " rethinking of the frameworks that should facilitate effective international cooperation . " <p> He stressed the importance of ensuring @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ to new challenges and create " global mechanisms that can respond to environmental , public health , cultural and social issues , as well as artificial intelligence . " <p> All of these developments must keep respect for fundamental human rights , social rights , and care for the environment in mind . <p> An exhortation and a planReferencing Pope Francis ' Apostolic Exhortation Laudate Deum , Cardinal Parolin warned against a " global authority concentrated in one person or in an elite with excessive power . " <p> Any future reforms to world governments must take into account the principles of subsidiarity and equal participation , so that , as the Pope wrote , effective global rules " can permit ' providing for ' this global safeguarding .
