@@117923194 <h> Chinese , British scientists help tea farmers tackle climate change <p> Photo from Yunnan University of Finance and Economics shows Chinese and British scientists discussing issues in a tea garden in Fengqing County , southwest China 's Yunnan Province on Nov 10 , 2024. -- Xinhua photo <p> KUNMING ( Nov 22 ) : Li Shaojuan , a female meteorologist , has been regularly visiting muddy tea plantations in China 's southwestern mountainous region over the past decade , in her bid to help local farmers cope with climate challenges . <p> Li , a professor at Yunnan University of Finance and Economics , visits the plantations in Fengqing and Baoshan counties , Yunnan Province , four to five times a year to do field studies aimed at better climate forecasting and service solutions . <p> As one of the core origins of tea trees in the world , Fengqing was part of the ancient Tea Horse Road , a trade route dating back to the Tang Dynasty ( 618-907 ) , and features tea gardens that are harvested three times a year -- in spring , summer and @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ in terms of both the quality and yield of its tea crops . <p> " Especially in spring , the likes of cold snaps , hail and drought make the most precious spring tea period unproductive , " said Zhang Guoqin , who runs a local tea company , adding that in summer , excessive rainfall fosters wet diseases , while higher temperatures and humidity fuel the spread of pests . <p> " We interviewed local tea farmers , and distributed questionnaires to them , hoping to understand their specific needs in detail , " Li said . <p> The scope of the interviews also included consumers , tea processing factories , refining factories , dealers , trade companies and more . <p> Li is among a group of Chinese and British atmospheric scientists who aim to use their expertise to provide tea farmers with more accurate weather forecasting services -- and help them better adapt to frequent meteorological disasters . <p> These scientists have developed mathematical models based on data revealing meteorological factors that affect tea tree growth and tea production . <p> " We need to understand how @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ type of weather forecasting to provide , " Li explained . <p> Annual predictions of both tea yield and quality can guide farmers in purchasing agricultural insurance to protect their income and ensure a stable market supply . <p> Subseasonal-to-seasonal forecasts can help farmers determine the optimal timing and strategies for activities such as harvesting fresh leaves , initial processing , fertilisation , and pest and disease management , she said . <p> However , this process is far from straightforward . Li revealed that inconsistencies between their initial research and real-world conditions often require them to revisit their models or conduct additional field studies . <p> " It 's a challenging process , but also one full of joy , because scientific research is about the constant pursuit of truth . " <p> Li 's work is part of a larger collaboration between China and the UK . <p> The Climate Science for Service Partnership China ( CSSP China ) project , launched in 2014 by the UK Met Office , the China Meteorological Administration , and the Institute of Atmospheric Physics ( IAP ) of the Chinese Academy @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ and social development . <p> With more countries facing extreme climate events , there is an urgent need to improve international collaboration and build services to enhance society 's resilience in the face of the impacts of climate change , Rowan Sutton , director of the UK Met Office Hadley Centre for Climate Science and Services , said at the CSSP China annual workshop held early this month . <p> So far , this initiative has developed several climate service prototypes , including seasonal rainfall forecasts for the Yangtze River Basin , the maize yield in northeastern China , and the tea industry in Yunnan Province . <p> Photo from the Institute of Atmospheric Physics ( IAP ) of the Chinese Academy of Sciences shows tea trees at a tea garden in Fengqing County , southwest China 's Yunnan Province on Aug 7 , 2024. -- Xinhua photo <p> Li 's research in Yunnan Province is being complemented by similar studies in the UK . <p> " We 're conducting case studies in Devon and Scotland , and we hope to complement our research by doing comparative studies between the @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ environments , " said Stacey New , an expert from the UK Met Office . <p> The team has found that the impacts of climate change on the tea industry vary widely depending on location , elevation and farming practices . <p> " Therefore , there is no one-size-fits-all solution for climate change adaptation in the tea sector , and climate services need to be tailored and customised to the specific circumstances and needs of the stakeholders , " said New . <p> The team 's research results have been detailed in a paper recently published in the journal Advances in Atmospheric Sciences . <p> " The CSSP China project is a good example of Sino-British cooperation to jointly address climate change , " said Zhou Tianjun , deputy director of the IAP , adding that the experience gained could be expanded to other tea-growing regions and may even apply to other crops like coffee and sugarcane . <p> In addition to weather forecasting , Li has also been looking into ancient farming practices in her quest for answers . <p> She recently discovered an ancient tea plantation in southwest @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ old but still produce harvestable leaves of good quality . <p> What sets this plantation apart is its natural and biodiverse ecosystem , which has made it more resilient to climate extremes . <p> For example , among the diverse tree species in the ancient plantation , the alder tree plays a key role as a nitrogen-fixing plant , enhancing soil fertility . <p> Additionally , the ground beneath tea trees there is typically covered with grass and a thick layer of humus , which helps retain moisture and regulate temperature , enhancing tea trees ' resilience to adverse weather conditions , she said . <p> Li believes this ancient approach could hold valuable insights for modern tea farmers . <p> " The ancient tea garden is an ecosystem that resists climate change , " she said . <p> " I hope to integrate this wisdom into today @@117985101 <h> There is no time for indifference today <p> As the world confronts the escalating crisis of climate change , Pope Francis has once again used his influential voice to urge global leaders to prioritise the well-being of humanity and the preservation of our shared planet . <h> Nov 22 , 2024 <h> Related News <p> By Patricia PereiraAs the world confronts the escalating crisis of climate change , Pope Francis has once again used his influential voice to urge global leaders to prioritise the well-being of humanity and the preservation of our shared planet . In a message delivered on November 13 by Cardinal Pietro Parolin at COP29 in Baku , Azerbaijan , the Pope emphasised the critical need for a united global response , especially in the face of growing disillusionment with multilateral institutions and the rise of dangerous isolationist trends . <p> Pope Francis ' message comes at a critical juncture . The absence of key world leaders -- such as US President Joe Biden , Chinese President Xi Jinping , French President Emmanuel Macron , and Brazil 's Luiz Inacio Lula da Silva -- from the @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Yet , the Pope 's message cut through this uncertainty with clarity and conviction : the time for inaction has passed . " Selfishness -- individual , national , and of power groups -- feeds a climate of mistrust and division , " the Pope warned , highlighting the pressing need for collective action in an increasingly interdependent world . <p> This lack of unity is especially troubling when weighed against the overwhelming scientific evidence demanding urgent action on climate change . Pope Francis , a long-standing advocate for environmental justice through his landmark encyclical Laudato Si ' , once again reminded the global community of its responsibility . " The scientific data available to us do not allow any further delay , " he cautioned , underscoring the need to limit global warming to 1.5 ? C by achieving net-zero CO2 emissions by the end of the 2030s . This goal , he stressed , is not just an environmental concern but a moral imperative tied to humanity 's survival . <p> The Pope also drew a direct connection between environmental degradation and global peace , emphasising that failing @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ His call for action is especially poignant in light of the growing political and social divisions around the world . As nations increasingly turn inward , prioritising short-term gains and self-interest , Pope Francis called for a renewed sense of global solidarity . " We should act and live as members of one family inhabiting the same interconnected global village , " he reminded world leaders , pointing to the shared fate of all humanity . <p> One of the most pressing issues at COP29 is financing climate action , particularly for developing nations that suffer the worst impacts of climate disasters despite contributing the least to the problem . The Pope reiterated his longstanding plea for the cancellation of debts for the world 's poorest countries , a point he previously raised in his papal bull Spes Non Confundit ( Hope Does Not Disappoint ) . He framed debt cancellation not as an act of charity but as a matter of justice . " There is a true ' ecological debt ' from the North to the South , " the Pope declared , drawing attention to the disproportionate @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ . <p> This concept of ecological debt is closely linked to global trade imbalances and the exploitation of resources , which have left many poorer nations vulnerable to both environmental and economic crises . In his message , the Pope called for a " new international financial architecture " that would enable all countries -- especially those most vulnerable to climate change -- to pursue lowcarbon development pathways that respect human dignity and promote equitable growth . Such a system , he argued , is essential for addressing the deep-rooted inequalities that fuel the climate crisis . <p> Pope Francis ' message to COP29 participants was not just a call for justice but also a challenge to the complacency that often paralyses meaningful action on climate change . " We can not wash our hands of it , with distance , with carelessness , with disinterest , " he said , urging world leaders to confront the mechanisms of self-justification that allow inaction to persist . His appeal was particularly aimed at those who downplay the urgency of climate change or delay critical action for political or economic reasons . @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ of the climate crisis , warning , " There is no time for indifference today . " He stressed that we can not absolve ourselves of this responsibility , urging world leaders and individuals alike to confront the self-deception that allows us to ignore the problem . The true challenge of our century , he suggested , is not only to mitigate climate change but to overcome the complacency that stalls meaningful action . As climate disasters become more frequent and severe , the Pope 's message serves as a moral wake-up call : short-term interests can no longer take precedence over the long-term survival of our planet . His call to action at COP29 challenges each of us -- whether world leader or ordinary citizen -- to take responsibility for the future of our common home .

This feature is also subject to the insurer 's terms . <p> A householder policy would compensate for losses from fire , lightning , explosions , and natural disasters . -- Picture by Farhan Najib <p> Householder Policy <p> Also known as personal property insurance or a home content policy , this insurance protects the contents of your property , including furniture , built-in fixtures , documents , appliances , jewellery , and valuable artwork . <p> @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ property 's contents due to incidents such as fire , lightning , explosions , natural disasters , impact , or theft involving forcible entry . <p> It also covers the loss of use of insured items and offers options to include personal liability and personal accident coverage . <p> However , policyholders must list the items they wish to insure . Compensation may not always reflect the full value of these items , as depreciation is often factored in . <p> To facilitate claims , proof of ownership and item values , such as receipts and photographs , are required . <p> For properties at risk of natural disasters like floods , investing in home insurance is essential to help rebuild after a catastrophe . <p> Some insurers in Malaysia offer comprehensive policies that combine homeowner and householder insurance , providing broader coverage under a single plan . @@118043140 <p> BAKU : The COP29 summit in Azerbaijan has reached an agreement to provide at least US$1.3 trillion annually through 2035 to poorer countries to help them fight climate change , with at least US$300 billion to come from mainly industrialised nations , the German news agency dpa reported . <p> The funding is intended to help developing countries enhance climate protection and adapt to the devastating effects of global warming , such as more frequent droughts , storms , and floods . <p> Currently , long-standing industrial nations mobilise over US$100 billion annually in climate aid . However , according to an independent UN expert group , the need for external assistance now stands at around US$1 trillion per year until 2030 , and even US$1.3 trillion by 2035 . <p> To raise the US$1.3 trillion annually , the agreement stipulates that multilateral development banks should significantly increase their lending or cancel debts for poorer countries . Public funds and bank resources should leverage private investments on a large scale , which will also be counted as climate financing . <p> Moreover , additional donor countries are encouraged to @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ climate activists criticised the plan because no specific entity is held accountable to reach the global goal . <p> Germany , like all other nations , is not specifically obliged to make payments of certain amounts by the resolution . <p> Ultimately , a compromise was reached , partly because it remains somewhat unclear how the trillion-dollar sum will be sourced -- this task will now fall to the next climate conference in Brazil . -- BERNAMA-dpa

@@118078745 <p> SABAH boasts Malaysia 's longest coastline , around 1,700 km . If we add the coastlines of its hundreds of islands , the total length exceeds 4,000 km . Our coastline landscape is amazingly diverse and beautiful , from world class sandy beach to mangroves , from headland forest to cliff , from mudflat to mangroves . <p> Advertisement <p> Managing such extensive coastline both presents us with unique challenges and valuable opportunities to learn and master the art of sustainable development . At the core of this lie the principle of Build with Nature . <p> Build with nature as she is the " wisest engineer " and our guardian against climate change <p> While human civilization and engineering prowess have been around for only a few thousand years , nature has been engineering and shaping ( and reshaping ) planet Earth for billions of years . <p> Advertisement <p> As advanced as we consider our engineering skills are , we can not outsmart nature . In fact , a few hundred years after the scientific and industrial revolutions , we have come to realize that building and @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ sustainable future as we navigate the uncertainties of climate change . <p> Coastal erosion and flooding <p> Advertisement <p> Among the areas most heavily impacted by climate change are coastlines , where land meets sea . As a coastal engineer , let me walk you through the two major risks -- coastal erosion and flooding -- faced by Sabah 's coastline and how we can collectively and sustainably manage them . Coastal erosion deserves greater public attention , as it is often overshadowed by coastal flooding . <p> To complicate matters , these two risks " complement " each other : erosion exacerbates flooding , and vice versa . <p> For the longest time , natural forces -- waves , currents , and winds -- have been shaping our coastline . These forces vary throughout the years . Erosion ( loss of coastline ) and accretion ( gain of coastline ) are part of a natural coastal process governed by these forces and , more recently , human intervention . Coastal flooding is another natural phenomenon affecting our coastline , often occurring due to a combination of high tides , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ . <p> As the coastline erodes , higher water levels and larger waves can penetrate further inland , putting more property and lives at risk and intensifying the erosion . Coastal erosion and flooding affect Sabah in various ways , with different levels of severity and associated risks . Often , human activities contribute to worsening these problems , as we fail to " understand and prioritize working with " nature . <p> Be resilient or fail <p> We are not resilient enough in combating and managing these risks in the face of climate change . If we fail , our economic and social progress could be undone , along with the potential for an environmental catastrophe . We often heard about the coastal and climate change crisis faced by countries such as Maldives , Fiji , Tuvalu , and Kiribati . <p> Closer at home , crisis is already brewing along our coastline , for example , at Pulau Mantanani where fish bombing , unsustainable fishing practices , severe erosion and flooding are threatening our communities . In the near future , climate change might affect our hydropower generation @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ and cities . The economic lost is unimaginable . To be resilient , we need to innovate . <p> Traditional coastal engineering <p> Traditional coastal engineering relies on building structures such as breakwaters , seawalls , and groynes to alter and stabilize coastlines for human development . While this approach meets immediate needs , it often disrupts the overall coastline stability . <p> For example , groynes and seawalls block the natural movement of sediments along the coast , which are essential for maintaining coastline formation . When sediment supply is disrupted , the downdrift part of the coastline erodes , continuing until it reaches a new equilibrium -- by which time significant land , property , and even lives could be at risk . <p> Building with nature <p> Building with nature emphasizes observing and understanding how nature functions , then designing developments to fit in harmoniously . Instead of fighting natural forces , we work alongside them -- and where possible , leverage on them . For example , in areas with the right environmental conditions , mangroves , coral reefs , and seagrasses can be " built @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ providing natural protection against waves , currents , and winds . <p> Nature has , in fact , been protecting us all along . The 2004 tsunami disaster , for instance , showed that communities with dense mangrove forests suffered far less damage , as the mangroves dissipated the energy of the tsunami waves . <p> By assessing the economic benefits provided by natural protections , we can justify maintaining and enriching these natural assets . Leveraging these resources brings added environmental and social benefits , such as marine biodiversity , fishery stock replenishment , wildlife restoration , and eco-tourism , thereby boosting the development 's image and reputation . <p> Big elephant in the room -- climate change <p> The big , looming issue behind erosion and flooding is climate change . While sea-level rise is a widely discussed consequence , the broader impacts on weather and coastal systems are less understood . <p> Winds , waves , and rainfall patterns may change further in the coming decades , and our current knowledge and technologies can not accurately predict the future trends . Drought-stricken areas may experience floods and @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ severe and unpredictable , as we have already witnessed in Sabah and globally . With changing sea states , coastal erosion and flooding trends will also shift . What should our response be ? <p> Climate change adaptation and nature based solutions <p> Heavily fortifying coastlines against climate change impacts with hard engineering structures , like seawalls , is not a cost-effective nor environmentally friendly approach . Instead , this measure should be reserved for critical and environmentally less sensitive areas . On a larger scale , we should adopt a climate change adaptation approach that encourages innovative planning , designing , and modifying coastal development practices to reduce coastal vulnerability . <p> Prioritizing building with nature is essential to this adaptation . We can also combine nature-based solutions ( mangroves , swamps , wetlands , coral reefs , seagrasses , mudflats ) with hard engineering structures to create hybrid solutions . One successful example is integrating natural storage capabilities ( like forests and wetlands ) with engineering tools ( such as pumps and drainage systems ) in mitigating urban flooding while also providing environmental and recreational benefits for communities @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ adaptable to changing climatic conditions , unlike rigid engineering structures . If habitat removal is unavoidable , offsetting its impact through transplantation or replanting should be a project approval condition . <p> Adaptation at all levels of governance and investments <p> Climate change adaptation should not rest solely on governments , scientists , and engineers . Its success requires the collective effort along with a paradigm shift in how we develop coastlines . <p> Sabah is on the right track , with initiatives from the state , municipalities , and communities , such as the Sabah Maju Jaya 's Blue Economy Agenda , the formation of the Sabah Climate Action Council ( SCAC ) , Kota Kinabalu City Hall ( DBKK ) ' s Implementation , Monitoring and Evaluation Report for Climate Change Actions ( 2024 -- 2030 ) , and Pulau Banggi 's Climate Change Adaptation Action Committee . However , these efforts are not sufficient . <p> Our strategies must be forward-thinking and innovative -- from education and public awareness to policy development , from talent development to R&amp;D and investment in green technology . Climate change adaptation @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ KPIs ) for evaluating government agencies , government-linked companies ( GLCs ) , local authorities , education institutions , private businesses , and NGOs . <p> The KPIs shall cover the tri-dimensions of economic , environmental , and socio . Major investment decisions and project development must evaluate the consequences of climate change and implement adaptation measures . <p> Leave no one behind <p> More importantly , it is high time now that climate change adaptation and nature preservation to be listed in the manifesto of all political parties . We , the voters , shall made this applicable and accountable to all current and future leaders . <p> With time running out , our climate change adaptation and build-with-nature initiatives must accelerate and expand across all sectors , regions , and demographics . No one should be left behind in our collective mission to adapt to climate change and live in harmony with nature . <p> As a coastal engineer , Jack has spent the last 15 years looking at our coastline . @@118079021 <h> Navigating challenges of and solutions to EV Safety <p> Electric vehicles ( EVs ) have become the symbol of a cleaner , greener future . <p> Governments across the globe , supported by environmental activists , are championing their use to curb carbon emissions and mitigate climate change . <p> Conferences , seminars , and workshops tirelessly promote the benefits of EVs , emphasizing their role in reducing global reliance on fossil fuels.Yet , as we embrace these zero-emission vehicles , new challenges emerge -- especially concerning safety and fire risks . <p> The lithium-ion batteries powering these vehicles , while innovative and efficient , introduce vulnerabilities that demand our attention . <p> The reality is that EV fires , although rare , are particularly challenging to manage . <p> Lithium-ion batteries can experience what is known as a " thermal runaway . " This phenomenon occurs when uncontrolled heat release triggers a self-sustaining chain reaction , potentially leading to fires or even explosions . 
@@118122925 <p> This move is part of a broader strategy to phase out NGVs nationwide , reflecting Malaysia 's distinct priorities in transportation policy . <p> While NGVs are often regarded internationally as a cleaner alternative to conventional fuels , Malaysia 's decision underscores its focus on public safety and sustainable development . <p> The policy arises from concerns over aging NGV infrastructure , with many vehicles in Malaysia utilizing tanks that exceed their 15-year lifespan , increasing risks of leaks and accidents . This ban aims to protect both vehicle users and the broader public from such hazards while signaling Malaysia 's commitment to redefining its approach to clean and safe transportation . <p> In Malaysia , a significant portion of the NGV fleet has already exceeded or is approaching this critical threshold , leading to increased road risks . Countries with newer NGV fleets or more rigorous inspection systems may not face the same urgency , making Malaysia 's decision a response to unique safety concerns . <p> Additionally , ensuring consistent and thorough inspections for NGVs across Malaysia can be a logistical challenge , particularly in less accessible @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ aims to mitigate safety risks more efficiently than by attempting to enforce comprehensive inspection protocols across the entire country . <p> The phase-out prioritizes public safety , addressing potential dangers that could arise from aging NGV vehicles . <p> As an oil and gas-producing nation , Malaysia 's energy policies are closely tied to its economic priorities . The petroleum sector plays a central role in the nation 's economy , and the move away from NGVs helps align Malaysia 's energy policies with its broader economic interests . By phasing out NGVs , Malaysia opens the door to promoting alternative energy sources that offer more long-term economic benefits , contributing to a diversified energy landscape . <p> The scarcity of NGV replacement parts , especially tanks , also poses a significant challenge . The rising costs and limited availability of NGV components have led to increased repair expenses , with some vehicle owners resorting to unsafe modifications or unreliable parts to maintain their vehicles . This trend places a financial burden on NGV owners while compromising public safety . Unlike countries with more established NGV markets and stable parts @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ economically unfeasible to maintain NGV infrastructure . <p> Thus , the ban is a practical step toward supporting safer and more economically viable transportation alternatives . <p> Environmental sustainability is a key consideration behind Malaysia 's decision to phase out natural gas vehicles ( NGVs ) . While NGVs emit fewer pollutants than traditional petrol or diesel vehicles , they fall short compared to the superior environmental benefits of electric vehicles ( EVs ) and hybrid alternatives . <p> Malaysia demonstrates its strong commitment to combating climate change through its goal to reduce greenhouse gas ( GHG ) emissions intensity by 45% by 2030 relative to 2005 levels . This target aligns with Malaysia 's obligations under the Paris Agreement and its long-term vision of achieving carbon neutrality by 2050 . <p> To accomplish these objectives , Malaysia is advancing multiple initiatives , including enhancing energy efficiency , accelerating the adoption of renewable energy , and employing carbon capture technologies . These measures are crucial in fostering sustainable transportation and supporting the nation 's transition to a low-carbon economy . <p> The decision to phase out natural gas vehicles ( @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ It reflects a commitment to embracing cleaner energy sources , such as electric vehicles ( EVs ) , as part of global efforts to mitigate climate change . While NGVs might be considered adequate within some countries ' emissions standards , Malaysia 's policy represents a proactive approach and a higher environmental standard , emphasizing the urgency of meeting its climate targets and positioning itself as a leader in sustainable energy practices . <p> NGVs make up only about 0.2% of Malaysia 's vehicle fleet , a relatively small share that reflects low market demand . This limited adoption has resulted in a shrinking NGV market , with fewer vehicles on the road and diminishing infrastructure needs . In contrast to countries with larger NGV markets , where a ban would cause significant disruptions , Malaysia 's modest NGV presence allows for a smoother transition . <p> Furthermore , Malaysia has not heavily invested in NGV refueling stations , leaving the infrastructure underdeveloped . The government sees little justification for continuing to invest in a diminishing system , instead choosing to redirect resources toward the development of EV charging stations @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ transition , Petronas , Malaysia 's national oil and gas company , has launched the Petronas NGV Transition Assistance Program . <p> This initiative offers financial support to individuals , particularly taxi drivers , who rely on NGVs for their livelihoods . Petronas provides subsidies for vehicle conversions or assistance with purchasing alternative vehicles , demonstrating a socially responsible approach to the ban . This support helps mitigate the economic impact on those affected , ensuring a smoother transition away from NGVs . <p> Unlike other countries that may hesitate to implement a ban without a robust support framework , Malaysia 's commitment to assisting impacted individuals showcases a balanced approach , addressing both safety and sustainability while providing necessary support to those affected by the phase-out . <p> Malaysia 's decision to phase out NGVs is driven by a combination of safety concerns , economic considerations , environmental objectives , and the limited market demand for NGVs . The aging fleet , the scarcity of parts , and the alignment with Malaysia 's sustainability goals make a compelling case for this policy . As an oil-producing nation with ambitious @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ strategic move toward safer , cleaner , and more economically sustainable transportation . Through initiatives like the Petronas NGV Transition Assistance Program , Malaysia demonstrates its commitment to a balanced transition , prioritizing safety and sustainability while addressing the potential social and economic impacts . <p> Ultimately , the NGV ban reflects Malaysia 's vision for a safer , more environmentally friendly future , aligned with both global sustainability trends and its economic interests . This unique approach distinguishes Malaysia from other nations , offering a forward-thinking model for energy and transportation policy .

@@118148893 <h> KRI : As rich states fail to reduce planet warming , Malaysia must take fight to global stage <p> New to Versa ? It 's an app designed to help you grow your savings easily . Malaysia has been upping its campaign to reduce single-up plastics , but according to KRI 's report , more needs to be done at a faster pace to save the planet . -- AFP pic <p> Recommendations for Malaysia <p> The think tank said that Malaysia may have taken some policy missteps that @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ it waived the external conditionality in its Nationally Determined Contribution ( NDC ) to the Paris Agreement set ahead of the Glasgow climate summit in 2021 , a move researchers said may have given the wrong signal that it was a developing country that does not need climate-related financial assistance . <p> " Malaysian policy is currently focused on signalling high mitigation ambition ; it has no hedging strategy , " the KRI researchers said . <p> " Care must be taken when committing to substantial financial expenditure or a major shift in energy security policy that they do not result in unacceptable curtailment of Malaysia 's limited state power and development potential , " they added . <p> KRI said Malaysia would need to quickly adapt , urging Putrajaya to calibrate its climate strategy " to signal both constructive international action and hedge against risk of system failure . " <p> Climate activists demonstrate against oil and gas corporations and to end fossil fuels outside the Cape Town International Convention Centre during the Southern Africa Oil and Gas Conference on September 13 , 2023. -- Reuters pic <p> COP29 @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ , Japan and Russia alone account for half the world 's GHGs , and there is a global consensus that they should bear much of the financial costs to tackle the fallout from climate change . <p> Over the weekend , rich countries pledged to contribute US$300 billion ( about RM1.3 trillion in current exchange rate ) a year by 2035 after two weeks of intense negotiations at the United Nations climate summit ( COP29 ) , where some 200 nations gathered in Azerbaijan 's capital , Baku . <p> However , the deal has been sharply criticised by many climate activists . <p> Developing nations said the amount is far below what is adequate to help poorer nations repair and build infrastructures and put in place policies to mitigate and adapt to global warming . <p> Despite the adoption of a broader target of US$1.3 trillion annually by 2035 , only US$300 billion was designated for grants and low-interest loans , most of it expected to be delivered by the private sector . <p> South-east Asia is one of the most climate-vulnerable regions and in Malaysia , global warming @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ billions of ringgit in property damages and damaging food crops . <p> In response , subsequent administrations to formulate climate policies that analysts said are mostly centered on mitigation and carbon emissions , but often failing to account for adaptation and financing for damages and losses . <p> Malaysia 's coastal cities and towns also face dangers from rising sea water levels , a hazard KRI said has not been dealt with properly in Putrajaya 's climate policy . <p> " Malaysia is a highly coastal country with 70 per cent of its population in coastal zones . Critical economic infrastructure such as Port Klang , Malaysia 's international trading gateway , lie in areas at risk of sea-level rise ( but ) research is lacking on the quantification of adaptation interventions needed as opposed to modelling of impacts , " it said . <p> The disappointing outcome from COP29 came as more climate analysts and policymakers from developing nations have become sceptical that the Paris Agreement 's 1.5 ? C warming target would be met , which they blamed on continued geopolitical tension and rich countries ' evasive attitude towards decarbonisation. 
@@118149320 <h> Illnesses projected to surge as country gets warmer due to climate change <p> MALAYSIA 's combination of high temperatures and humidity places an added vulnerability towards heat-related illnesses and warmer temperatures can worsen other health issues for Malaysians , according to Khazanah Research Institute 's latest working paper Navigating Public Health in a Hotter Malaysia , which brings to light some of the public health challenges Malaysia could face as global temperatures increase . <p> Increased risks of heat-related illnesses <p> Heat is a critical and growing threat to human health . As global temperatures rise , instances of heat stress can become increasingly common . <p> The risks of heat stress can further worsen during instances of high heat and high humidity , which can then lead to the development of heat-related illnesses such as heat stroke , heat exhaustion and heat cramps . <p> Thus , Malaysia 's high humidity level ( ranging between 65% to 95% relative humidity ) and rising temperatures poses an increasing risk of these heat-related illnesses among Malaysians . <p> During the past 2015 -- 2016 El Nino cycle , Malaysia reported @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ two fatalities . Meanwhile , the recent 2023 -- 2024 El Nino occurrence proved to be more deadly , with five fatalities out of the 127 reported cases of heat-related illnesses . <p> Such incidences are expected to rise as Malaysia is projected to have higher mean and maximum temperatures as well as an increased frequency of days exceeding 35 . <p> Warmer temperatures exacerbate other health issues <p> Rising temperatures are not only a concern for heat-related illnesses but they can also worsen a myriad of other health conditions . Particularly , higher temperatures have been shown to adversely impact cardiometabolic , respiratory conditions and other non-communicable diseases ( NCDs ) . Thus as temperatures are projected to rise , the strain NCDs have on healthcare resources is also likely to increase unless proper climate-health policies are in place . <p> Another public health concern that is brought on by rising temperatures is the rise in vector-borne diseases such as dengue , malaria and chikungunya . Various literature exploring the relationship between dengue and temperature has found that rising minimum temperatures can lead to an increase in dengue cases @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ <p> Vulnerability of heat-induced health issues not equal <p> Children , particularly those under the age of five , are more vulnerable to heat stress . During the 2023 -- 2024 El Nino event , six children aged 12 and below were reported to have heat-related illnesses . Out of these six cases , three resulted in fatalities . All three of these mortalities recorded were among children under the age of five . This underscores the added vulnerability children face in managing heat stress compared with adult . <p> While all Malaysians will experience warmer temperatures because of climate change , the degree to which they are vulnerable to heat-related health issues can vary depending on their sensitivity and adaptive capacity . Local temperature variations , age-related vulnerabilities among children and the elderly , and employment in particular industries can increase the risk of heat-related illnesses or other heat-exacerbated medical disorders . <p> Growing need to adapt to rising temperatures <p> The findings from this paper highlights Malaysia 's public health risks induced by warmer temperatures . To overcome these risks , this paper brings forward four policy recommendations @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ is imperative to manage heat-related impacts , particularly on health . <p> * Enhancing the monitoring of heat-related illnesses , hospital admissions and mortality rates as it is essential to understand the full impact of extreme heat events . <p> * Developing heat-sensitive public infrastructure and facilities in Malaysia is crucial to enhancing resilience against extreme heat . <p> * Adapting decent work practices to the rising temperatures is needed to protect worker safety and maintain productivity in a warming climate . 
@@118149339 <p> WORLD AMR ( Antimicrobial Resistance ) Awareness Week , celebrated annually from Nov 18 to 24 , is an initiative led by the World Health Organisation ( WHO ) to raise global awareness about antimicrobial resistance and encourage best practices to curb its spread . <p> AMR occurs when microorganisms evolve to resist the effects of medications designed to kill them , such as antibiotics , antivirals , antifungals and antiparasitics . This phenomenon poses a significant global health threat as it can lead to longer hospital stays , increased medical costs and higher mortality rates . <p> One alarming factor contributing to the rise of AMR is climate change , which disrupts ecosystems and human health dynamics , thereby increasing the infection rate . <p> Climate change can worsen AMR in several ways . It alters ecosystems by shifting habitats , which helps bacteria , viruses and parasites spread as temperatures rise . Warmer temperatures and extreme weather can extend the range of disease-carrying vectors , like mosquitoes , leading to more infections such as malaria and dengue . <p> Additionally , climate change can push wildlife closer @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ . As pathogens transfer from animals to humans crossing the species barrier , the likelihood of AMR also increases . <p> Water and food supply issues due to climate-related disruptions can create unsanitary conditions , raising the risk of infections . Moreover , using antibiotics in livestock for growth can lead to resistance that may be passed to humans . <p> To tackle AMR as a community , several proactive steps can be taken : <p> Collaboration with health authorities : Work with healthcare providers and local health authorities to ensure responsible antibiotic prescriptions , and educate patients on proper use . <p> Collaboration with school authorities : Work with school authorities to engage students in workshops on the prudent use of antibiotics , the importance of hygiene and sanitisation . <p> Improve hygiene and sanitation : Enhance sanitation and hygiene in homes and public places to limit infection spread . Simple actions like handwashing and safe food preparation can make a big difference . <p> Public awareness campaigns : Educate the public about AMR and its risks , dispelling myths and promoting responsible antibiotic use through workshops , information @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ : Encourage local institutions to invest in research on AMR and develop new treatments for lasting solutions . <p> Support sustainable practices : Encourage farming methods that reduce antibiotic use in livestock , and support local farms that prioritise animal welfare . <p> Healthcare professionals play an important role in tackling AMR . Effective training in antimicrobial stewardship is essential to promote the responsible use of antimicrobial drugs . <p> Using digital tools such as online courses , webinars and apps can make AMR education more accessible for healthcare providers . <p> Patient education during medical visits is important too , particularly in explaining when antibiotics are necessary and the risks of misuse . <p> Healthcare professionals can also work with policymakers to support regulations promoting antibiotic stewardship , AMR research funding and public health initiatives . <p> The Malaysian Action Plan on Antimicrobial Resistance 2022-2026 emphasises the collaboration between government agencies , healthcare providers , educational institutions and the private sector . <p> This comprehensive , long-term strategy aims to address AMR through a " one health " approach that safeguards public health , food security and environmental health @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ collective action at community , national and global levels , we can tackle AMR threat and ensure the continued efficacy of life-saving medications for the next generation . <p> DrPriya Madhavan is an associate professor at the School of Medicine , Faculty of Health and Medical Sciences , Taylor 's University . She also holds the position of hub leader of Communicable Diseases under Digital Health and Medical Advancement Impact Lab at the institution . Comments : letters@thesundaily.com

@@118167360 <p> PUTRAJAYA : Malaysia and South Korea signed a memorandum of understanding ( MoU ) to strengthen cooperation in addressing climate change under Article 6 of the Paris Agreement on Nov 25 , according to the Ministry of Natural Resources and Environmental Sustainability ( NRES ) in a statement today . <p> It said Minister Nik Nazmi Nik Ahmad and South Korean Environment Minister Kim Wan-sup signed the MoU in Yongsan , Seoul , adding that the exchange of documents was conducted by Foreign Minister Datuk Seri Mohamad Hasan , witnessed by Prime Minister Datuk Seri Anwar Ibrahim and South Korean President Yoon Suk Yeol . <p> The ministry said that the MoU enhances the commitment of both countries to achieve their Nationally Determined Contributions ( NDCs ) targets under the Paris Agreement and long-term aspirations while promoting sustainable development and environmental integrity . <p> It added that the MoU also aligns with the outcomes of the Article 6 negotiations adopted at the United Nations Climate Change Conference , 29th Conference of the Parties ( UNFCCC COP29 ) in Baku , Azerbaijan . <p> " Malaysia remains steadfast in its @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ with a focus on future low-carbon development that is resilient to climate change while fostering sustainable and green growth , " read the statement . <p> It said Malaysia-South Korea cooperation is expected to bring significant benefits , including opening up new opportunities to reduce greenhouse ( GHG ) emissions further , facilitating foreign investments , and allowing technology transfer . <p> It added this cooperation will enhance technical expertise and ensure a more robust and transparent approach to tackling climate change . -- Bernama

@@118193522 <p> PETALING JAYA : Malaysia aims to produce two million tonnes of hydrogen annually by 2030. , scaling up to 16 million tonnes by 2050 , under the Emission Driven Scenario ( EDS ) , according to Science , Technology and Innovation Minister Chang Lih Kang . <p> He said the successful implementation of the Hydrogen Economy and Technology Roadmap ( HETR ) will enable Malaysia to tap into this lucrative market , with potential revenue estimated to be at least RM905 billion by 2050 under the EDS . <p> " The global green hydrogen market is projected to reach a staggering US$189.19 billion ( RM840 billion ) by 2050 , with Asia-Pacific accounting for 43% of this market , followed by Asean with 13% , and Malaysia at 2% . As we transition to cleaner energy , hydrogen will play a key role alongside other renewables in Malaysia , " Chang stated in his keynote speech at FMM Energy Efficiency &amp; Conservation Conference 2024 organised by the Federation of Malaysian Manufacturers today . <p> He said embracing hydrogen is not just about tackling climate change but it is also @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ protecting publics ' health . " The shift will help decarbonise critical sectors like power generation and transportation , reducing greenhouse gas emissions by up to 10% . " <p> Through the HETR , Malaysia plans to phase out fossil-fuel-based grey hydrogen in the short term and transition towards green hydrogen in the long term . <p> " To bridge the gap , blue hydrogen will play a crucial role by leveraging existing fossil fuel infrastructure while incorporating carbon capture to reduce emissions , " Chang said . <p> From 2030 to 2040 , he added , efforts will focus on making green hydrogen more cost-competitive by improving technology and efficiency to pave for a sustainable future . " Blue hydrogen will be critical for industries like metal , steel , oil and gas refining , and ammonia production , where emissions are harder to reduce . " <p> However , Chang said , the main challenge is cost where hydrogen is currently twice the price of unsubsidised RON 97 fuel and much higher than subsidised RON 95 . <p> " While the HETR projects that hydrogen will become cheaper @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ it 's time to start redirecting energy subsidies towards renewables like hydrogen now . This gradual shift , alongside financial incentives for cleaner fuels , can help accelerate the transition towards a sustainable energy future , " he said . <p> Chang pointed to key strategies to accelerate the adoption of green hydrogen , including offering financial incentives such as Green Investment Tax Allowance and Green Income Tax Exemption for hydrogen-related projects , alongside e-dana support for innovation and commercialisation . <p> " Plans are also under way to make fuel cell electric vehicles more affordable through subsidies and tax exemptions on sales , imports , and road use . @@118211212 <h> Coral Nursery Site established at UMS <p> UMS ' Coral Nursery Site team placing cube-shaped blocks which serves as structures for corals to attach to and as shelter for fish . <p> KOTA lifeblood of marine ecosystems , not only providing habitat for many marine life species , but also playing an important role in protecting coastlines from erosion , supporting fishing activities and being a major attraction in the tourism sector . <p> Prof Kasim said acknowledging that unfortunately , human activities such as unsustainable development , pollution and climate change have caused serious destruction to the precious ecosystem , they are taking several proactive steps through the launching of the coral nursery . <p> Secondly , providing a study @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ understand the role and challenges of conserving marine ecosystems . <p> Thirdly , engaging local communities , non-governmental organisations , government agencies and universities in strategic collaboration to ensure the sustainability of marine resources . <p> " The Coral Nursery Site programme also supports the goals of the Coral Triangle Initiative ( CTI ) and the Sustainable Development Goals ( SDGs ) that we all strive for , in protecting underwater life and contributing to action to address climate change through the construction of artificial habitats . <p> " In addition , this programme also provides an opportunity for all of us to raise public awareness of the importance of preserving coral reefs as a valuable asset to our ecosystems and economies . <p> " Indeed , this effort is a shared <p> Around 450,000 deaths a year from heart disease were linked to fire-related air pollution between 2000 and 2019 , the researchers said . <p> A further 220,000 deaths from respiratory disease were China , the Democratic Republic of Congo , India , Indonesia , and Nigeria . <p> A record amount of illegal burning of farm fields in northern India has been partly blamed for noxious smog that has poor nations further highlights " climate injustice " , in which those who have contributed the least to global warming suffer from it the most , they added . <p> Some of the ways people can avoid With the stakes higher than ever , the final proposal called on wealthy nations to provide US$300 billion annually to help poorer nations transition to clean energy and adapt to climate change . <p> While this is a significant increase from the previous commitment of $100 billion , it falls far short of the $1 trillion estimated by experts to meet the needs of developing countries . <p> The fact that developing nations walked out over a previous proposal of $250 billion is telling of the deep divides at play . <p> The dissatisfaction is not unfounded . @@118292304 <h> Discovering curious creatures of the coral <p> Jorunna parva is one of the smallest inhabitants of coral reefs at a length of 1cm. -- Photo by Izuzuki Diver / Wikimedia Commons <p> THE recent COP29 meeting last month , held in Baku , Azerbaijan revealed that 44 per cent of reef building coral species are at risk of extinction caused by global warming and climate change occasioned by the burning of fossil fuels . Coral bleaching is the net result of the warming of our oceans and as the warming gathers pace , bleaching events will become more frequent as the corals expel their colourful symbiotic algae leaving them dead and whitish in colour . <p> This is already evident in Southeast Asia 's Coral Triangle , which is the most biologically marine environment in the world covering 6.8 million square km of the Pacific Ocean and bordering six countries ; Malaysia , Indonesia , the Philippines , Timor-Leste , Papua New Guinea , and the Solomon Islands . <p> A global temperature rise of two degrees Celsius above the norm will wipe out all corals in this ecologically @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ , which could well face extinction : sea bunnies , sea kraits , and bluefin trevally . <p> Sea bunnies ( Jorunna parva ) <p> This shell-less gastropod mollusc is one of the smallest inhabitants of coral reefs at a length of 1cm . It has been named ' bunny ' because of its two black and white rhinophores , which resemble rabbit ears . <p> Rhinophores are pairs of earlike structures which appear on the head of some of the sea slug family and are scent receptors . Slithering over coral reefs in tropical waters and along the seabed , these white , yellow , or green creatures have black papillae scattered over their bodies like small black spots . <p> They are found scattered across the Indo-Pacific Oceans , feeding on toxic sponges of the Chalinidae variety , the scent of which they detect through their rhinophores and thus they are seldom eaten by fish . If attacked by a nibbling fish , the sea bunny retracts its ears into pockets beneath its skin . <p> As hermaphrodites , producing both sperm and eggshells , they need to @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ and in this way the continuation of the species is maintained . <p> Sea kraits ( Laticauda laticaudata ) <p> These semi aquatic snakes are characterised by their scales , which they use when moving overland and by their vertical paddle shaped tails used for swimming . Their long bodies , growing up to 1.5 metres , possess regular black stripes interspersed with whitish-grey colourings . Found on coral reefs , scattered off South and Southeast Asian islands from India to Fiji and north to Japan , they are occasionally found on the Australian east coast and even in New Zealand . <p> Possessing a highly toxic venom , they prefer to hunt at night but are often seen by day and they can dive to depths of 80 metres , but they mostly live in shallow coastal waters allowing them access to the shore . On coral reefs they may be found slithering through the nooks and crannies , flushing out small fish which are rapidly gobbled up by the larger bluefin trevally fish , which trail the black banded sea kraits in packs ready to pounce on the @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ of conger and moray eels , squid , crabs , and small fish . Whilst snorkelling off coral reefs in the Indian Ocean and the South China Sea , I have seen many sea kraits and carefully avoided them for they possess a venomous bite , especially at nighttime or when suddenly grabbed and lifted out of the sea . <p> Kraits have been seen scaling limestone cliffs on the shoreline as they hit the land where they mate , with the female laying her eggs in nests . Little is known about their nests for these snakes are very shy creatures . <p> Bluefin trevally ( Caranx melampygus ) <p> This fish , also known as the ' blue finned crevalle ' , has a wide distribution in the Indian and Pacific Oceans . In 1833 , a French naturalist , Georges Cuvier , classified this fish from specimens caught off Waigio in Indonesia . <p> These are large fish reaching a length of about 80cm and weighing up to 44kg . With an oblong , compressed body , they have large dorsal fins and a pointed snout . @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ , whilst adult species live in the deeper waters on the outer reef slopes but have been seen diving to depths of 180 metres . Frequently they swarm around coral reefs attracted by a natural chemical released by the coral algae . <p> Feeding mostly on smaller fish , they supplement their diet with smaller quantities of shrimps and crabs , eating in total an average of 45kg of fish per annum . They have been seen individually , or in shoals of about 20 individuals , chasing smaller fish into the latter 's home amongst the corals or into the lagoons at high tide , departing at the start of the ebb tide with their bellies full . <p> As mentioned before , they coexist on the outer edges of coral reefs , with sea kraits which they follow , swooping down on smaller fish disturbed by the snake 's movement amongst the corals . <p> The bluefin trevally reaches sexual maturity in its second year when the male is nearly 35cm long and the female at 33cm . They are multiple spawners , often at over eight times @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ the third or fourth phases of the moon and at nighttime to avoid predators . Between 0.5 to 4 million eggs are released at each time . Once the larvae are hatched the juveniles slowly move from shallow waters to deeper reefs growing at an average rate of 0.45mm per day . <p> The main threats to this species of fish range from sharks and rays to commercial fishing activities by drift nets and hook and line as they are highly desirable and prized food . Currently , on the IUCN list , they are not a threatened species but with ever-dying coral reefs this may soon change ! <p> These little known but quite incredible creatures , I have had the great privilege of seeing , in my younger days , when snorkelling on coral reefs off islands and shores in the Indian and the western Pacific Oceans and still remember exactly what they looked like , always treasuring the identification of them by the local fishermen who took me to such amazing reefs .

September 5 , 2024. -- File pic via Reuters <p> WASHINGTON : US trade officials announced yesterday a new round of tariffs on solar panel imports from four Southeast Asian nations after American manufacturers complained that companies there are flooding the market with unfairly cheap goods . <p> It is the second of two preliminary decisions that President Joe Biden 's Commerce Department is making this year in a trade case brought by Korea 's Hanwha Qcells , Arizona-based First Solar Inc and several smaller producers seeking to protect billions of dollars in investments in US solar manufacturing . <p> Advertisement <p> The group , the American Alliance for Solar Manufacturing Trade Committee , accused big @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ , Vietnam and Thailand of causing global prices to collapse by dumping products into the market . <p> According to a preliminary decision posted on the US Commerce Department 's website on Friday , the agency calculated dumping duties of between 21.31 per cent and 271.2 per cent , depending on the company , on solar cells from Cambodia , Malaysia , Thailand and Vietnam . <p> Jinko Solar received duties of 21.31 per cent for products made in Malaysia and 56.51 per cent for those produced in Vietnam . <p> China 's Trina Solar received a dumping margin of 77.85 per cent for products it makes in Thailand and 54.46 per cent for those it produces in Vietnam . <p> Advertisement <p> In contrast , the Commerce Department did not lay out any dumping margin for Hanwha Qcells products made in Malaysia . <p> In October , the department had calculated subsidy rate of 14.72 per cent for the company . <p> Advertisement <p> The department 's final determinations are set for April 18 , 2025 , with the International Trade Administration set to finalise its determinations the following @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ " With these preliminary duties , we are moving closer to addressing years of harmful unfair trade and protecting billions of dollars of investment in new American solar manufacturing and supply chains , " said Tim Brightbill , partner at Wiley Rein and lead counsel to the petitioners . <p> Representatives for Jinko and Trina were not immediately available for comment . <p> Most solar panels installed in the US are made overseas , and some 80 per cent of imports come from the four nations targeted in the Commerce Department probe . <p> The Biden administration this year raised the alarm over China 's massive investment in factory capacity for clean energy goods . <p> Biden 's landmark climate change law , the Inflation Reduction Act , includes incentives for companies that produce clean energy equipment in the US -- a subsidy that has prompted a flurry of plans for new solar factories . <p> President-elect Donald Trump has called the Inflation Reduction Act too expensive , but also has said he plans to slap hefty tariffs on a range of sectors to protect American workers . <p> Dumping @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ at a price below its cost of production or lower than what it charges in its home country .
